    - `ELIGIBLE_SQL`: the §10.10 checklist of L3939 (A2 §5.4) as one static predicate over `score_targets t`: `t.bulk_operation_id = :b AND t.state = 'pending' AND (t.not_before IS NULL OR t.not_before <= now()) AND NOT is_fenced(t.job_id, t.llm_tuple_id) AND t.resume_draft_id IS NULL AND NOT <targets.RESUMABLE_DRAFT_SQL> (the fragment of Task 9 is correlated to this predicate's alias `t` through `a.target_id = t.id` and binds `:policy`, the active `evaluation_policy_id`, so each candidate row is tested against its own attempt history: no `score_drafts` row on any attempt of the target under the active policy, because a target whose online attempt stored a generation and then returned to `pending` on a transient critic failure is resumed by `load_context`, never batched and paid for again; Codex r4 B9, r5 B1) AND (t.lane IS NULL OR t.lane NOT IN ('batch_followup', 'score_now')) AND (t.state_reason IS NULL OR t.state_reason NOT IN ('batch_max_tokens', 'batch_invalid_output', 'batch_errored', 'batch_canceled', 'batch_expired', 'batch_missing')) AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM batch_items i WHERE i.target_id = t.id AND i.result_type <> 'awaiting') AND t.charged_attempts < 4 AND t.override = 'none'`; the order `availability = 'active' DESC, <previous tuple's score_mid> DESC NULLS LAST, t.similarity DESC NULLS LAST, t.id` (L3942; the previous tuple's `score_mid` through the job's newest other-tuple current instance).
    - `async prepare(conn, *, bulk_operation_id, llm_tuple_id, operation: OperationClaim, settings: Settings, fake_llm: bool, now) -> Prepared(submission_id, request_count, reserved_usd, hold_usd) | NotPrepared(reason: Literal["batches_disabled", "paused", "in_flight", "ambiguous", "below_minimum", "no_allocation", "tuple_superseded"])`, the Prepare transaction of L3951 in the schedule's order (Codex r1 #18, r2 B8–B10, B12); a `NotPrepared` at any step rolls the whole transaction back, so nothing of a batch that is not sent is ever written: 1. `active_config FOR SHARE` (the tuple must still be the active one and the bulk operation `active`, else `NotPrepared("tuple_superseded")`; the active execution config and evaluation policy for the attempts, L3813); 2. `cycles.pause_reason(conn, fake_llm=fake_llm)` → `NotPrepared("paused")` (the one admission guard, Task 11: an unverified schema, an auth failure, an unacknowledged bound violation, the cap or a global fence stop the batch branch exactly as they stop a cycle; Codex r1 #25); 3. `lock_operation_owner(conn, operation)` (the operation row under this execution's claim, step 5: a reclaimed execution is `FencedOut` here and prepares nothing); 4. the preconditions (`batch_bulk_rescore`, no in-flight or `submit_ambiguous` submission: the `batch_one_in_flight` index is the last word); 5. `ensure_month(conn, now)`, the month row (`lock_month`; Codex r4 B8) and the bulk row `FOR UPDATE` (steps 6–7) — **before** the submission row exists, because `batch_submissions.bulk_operation_id` is an immediate FK and the insert would take a `FOR KEY SHARE` lock on the bulk row ahead of the month lock, a cycle with a funded online reservation that holds the month row and wants the bulk row `FOR UPDATE` (Codex r3 B1); 6. `INSERT batch_submissions (state = 'preparing', llm_tuple_id, bulk_operation_id, operation_id = operation.operation_id, operation_claim_id = operation.claim_id, request_count = 1, request_hash = '', reserved_usd = 0, followup_held_usd = 0)` (the owner row: a new, uncommitted row that no other transaction can lock, so inserting it after the month row breaks no order, and its key-share on the bulk row is one this transaction already holds `FOR UPDATE`; the placeholders are overwritten in step 9); **under the month and bulk locks** the candidates are read by `ELIGIBLE_SQL`, cut to `batch_max_items` and to `BATCH_MAX_BYTES = 200 * 1024 * 1024` of rendered params (the provider's 256 MB, A2 B14; Deviation 27), then to the largest `n` whose cumulative worst case plus `n × hold` fits the allocation (`spent_usd + followup_held_usd + Σ worst + n × hold ≤ budget_usd`, each item's worst case from its own rendered bound), `n < batch_min_targets` → `NotPrepared("below_minimum")`, no fit at all → `NotPrepared("no_allocation")`; 7. the CAS on the targets, `pending → in_batch` through `transition_where(["pending"], "in_batch", where="t.id IN (SELECT id FROM score_targets WHERE id = ANY(CAST(:ids AS bigint[])) AND <ELIGIBLE_SQL over the subquery's row> FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED)", set_sql={"attempt_no": "t.attempt_no + 1", "lane": "'batch'", "batch_submission_id": ":s"}, returning=("id", "attempt_no"))`: the rows are locked and their `attempt_no` advanced **before** any dependent row exists, so no online claim can take `attempt_no + 1` first (`UNIQUE (target_id, attempt_no)`, L2290), and `SKIP LOCKED` means this statement never waits for a target row (a candidate claimed, cancelled or swept meanwhile is simply absent from the returned set); 8. the surviving count `m`: `m < batch_min_targets` (including `m = 0`) → `NotPrepared("below_minimum")`, which rolls back the CAS and the submission row together (Codex r2 B9: the minimum is re-applied after the CAS, and `request_count ≥ 1` can never be violated); 9. per returned target `INSERT score_attempts (mode = 'batch', outcome = 'running', execution_config_id, evaluation_policy_id, lane = 'batch')` and `INSERT batch_items (submission_id, target_id, attempt_no, custom_id = 't<target>_a<attempt>', hold_usd = hold)`, then `reserve_batch` with `BatchSubmissionOwner(submission_id, "preparing")` and `hold_usd` (its allocation check runs again under the locks it holds, steps 6–9 of the schedule: the month and bulk rows were taken in step 6 and are re-taken freely), `batch_items.llm_call_id` set (the deferred pair, L2572–2573), and `UPDATE batch_submissions SET request_count = m, request_hash = sha256(ordered (custom_id ‖ sha256(canonical params))), reserved_usd = Σ, followup_held_usd = m × hold`; 10. `bulk.add_hold(m × hold)`; 11. `ranking.bump`. The rendered params per item: `batch_request_body(node, prompt, max_tokens=<first stage>)` with the prefix cache marker kept (L6622), rendered with the tuple's `PromptVersion` (Task 4). **Why the targets come after the month row here** (the schedule's one stated exception, Deviation 38): the CAS uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so `prepare` never waits for a target row; an online transaction that holds a target and waits for the month row (`fail`, `reserve`) therefore cannot form a cycle with it, and `activate_tuple` and `budget_release`, which hold the month row before touching targets, are serialised with `prepare` on `active_config`.
    - `async submit_start(conn, submission_id, *, operation: OperationClaim, now)`, in schedule order (Codex r2 B7): `active_config FOR SHARE` (the tuple still active, the bulk operation `active`), then the operation owner (`lock_operation_owner`), then the submission row `FOR UPDATE` under `state = 'preparing' AND operation_claim_id = :claim` (the submission's `cancel_requested_at IS NULL`, else `abandon` instead: nothing obsolete is dispatched; Codex r1 #24), then `preparing → submit_started` (the `submit_started_at` changed-at) and `mark_batch_dispatched` (R6; the month row, then the ledger entries, then `charged_attempts + 1` on every target and the attempts `charged`); fault points `batch.before_submit_start` and `batch.after_submit_start`.
    - The three recording transactions share one shape, in schedule order (Codex r2 B7, B13): `active_config FOR SHARE` → the operation owner (`lock_operation_owner`) → the submission row `FOR UPDATE` under `state = 'submit_started' AND operation_claim_id = :claim` (the owner) → the month row and the ledger entries (`settle` / `release_never_dispatched`, which re-take `active_config` and the month row freely) → the bulk row (`release_item_hold`) → the attempts and targets → `ranking_state`. **Attempts move only while `running`**: every cleanup uses `transition_where("score_attempts", ["running"], <outcome>, where="id = ANY(CAST(:ids AS bigint[]))")` (zero rows is fine), so an attempt that supersession already ended `cancelled` keeps that outcome and no `TransitionConflict` can roll a settlement back; the accounting of an item never depends on the attempt's or the target's state. `async record_created(conn, submission_id, created: Created, *, operation, now)`: `submit_started → submitted` with `provider_batch_id`, `expires_at = created.created_at + 24 h` (L3954); when `cancel_requested_at` is set meanwhile (a confirm during the send), the worker calls `messages.batches.cancel` right after the commit (the carried-forward request, Codex r1 #24) and `batch_poll` retries it. `async record_rejected(conn, submission_id, rejected: Rejected, *, operation, now)`: `submit_started → submit_rejected`; every item entry `settle(outcome="not_billed", evidence="batch_submit_rejected", call=CallOutcome(status="error", http_status=rejected.status, error_code="batch_submit_rejected"))`; `bulk.release_item_hold(conn, item_id=…)` per item (Task 10); the attempts still `running` → `submit_rejected`; the targets `in_batch → pending` with `batch_submission_id` cleared (owner `batch_submission_id = :s AND attempt_no = :a`; a cancelled target is left as it is); the follow-on (below). `async record_ambiguous(conn, submission_id, ambiguous: Ambiguous, *, operation, now)`: `submit_started → submit_ambiguous` with `reconcile_report = {"error_class": …}`; nothing else moves (L3956). Fault point `batch.after_create` between the HTTP call and the recording transaction.
    - `async abandon(conn, submission_id, *, now)` (step 4d), in the recording shape above (`active_config` → the owner under `state = 'preparing'` → month → bulk → rows → ranking): `preparing → abandoned`; `release_never_dispatched` per item entry (the calls `cancelled`, the month and the bulk debit credited, Task 7); the item holds released (`bulk.release_item_hold` per item); the attempts still `running` → `cancelled` with `outcome_detail = 'batch_abandoned'` (A2 B10, Deviation 12; an attempt supersession already ended keeps its outcome, Codex r2 B13); the `in_batch` targets of the submission → `pending`; the follow-on. **Recovery of a lost execution** (Codex r1 #23, r2 B12, r3 B2, B3): `async recover_lost(conn, *, epoch: int, operation_id: int | None = None) -> Recovered(abandoned: int, ambiguous: int)` finds every `preparing` or `submit_started` submission whose stored execution is gone, by a predicate that needs no particular sweep order: `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM operations o WHERE o.id = s.operation_id AND o.status = 'running' AND o.claim_id = s.operation_claim_id AND o.worker_epoch >= :epoch)`, i.e. the operation is not `running`, or runs under a claim other than the one that prepared the submission (after a reclaim the restarted handler's own claim differs from the stored one, so it recognises its predecessor's rows), or belongs to an **older worker epoch** (`operations.worker_epoch`, `operations.py:287`, `:357`: a dead epoch's operation is recognised whether or not the `operations` sweep has already cleared its claim); it moves `preparing → abandoned` (as `abandon`) and `submit_started → submit_ambiguous` with `reconcile_report = {"error_class": "execution_lost"}`; a `submit_started` row is **never** resent, whether the kill came before or after the HTTP send: the ambiguous reconciler of Task 17 finds the batch if it was created, or the user releases it after 25 h. It runs at three places, each inside a transaction that holds `fence_epoch` and `active_config FOR SHARE` **before** any operation or submission row (the schedule): `register_takeover_sweep("batch_submissions")` (every such row of any operation, correct in either order relative to `"operations"`), which `claim_epoch_and_sweep` runs in a **transaction of its own** after the epoch claim committed, opened with `fence_epoch` and `active_config FOR SHARE` before the statement touches a submission or operation row (Task 27; Codex r4 B1: the `operations` sweep's `FOR UPDATE` rows live in another transaction that has already committed or not yet started, so no sweep's operation lock is ever held while this one asks for the configuration), the `batch_submit` handler's first transaction (its own operation's rows, before preparing anew), and `@reconciler("batch_submissions", "tick")`, which runs the same statement every minute after a lock-free read finds a candidate row (the `scope_apply_failed` pattern, `settings/scope_apply.py:65`: no `active_config` lock is taken on an empty pass) — **not** an operation failure hook, because a hook runs inside `finish_operation`'s transaction after the operation row is locked (`worker/operations.py:402–431`) and could not take `active_config` without inverting the schedule; an operation that ends `failed` therefore leaves its rows to the next tick, at most a minute away. An old execution that is still running after its reclaim is fenced by `lock_operation_owner` in `prepare`, `submit_start` and the recording transactions, so it can prepare and send nothing.
    - `async follow_on(conn, *, submission_id, defer) -> bool`, keyed by the **originating** submission (Codex r2 B15): it fires when that submission ends `submit_rejected`, `abandoned` or `reconciled` with `released_at IS NULL`, and for a `released` one at the release itself (L3945; "ends" means those four events, not `ended`: A2 B13); the adoption of a released submission (`released → ended → reconciled`) and any later replay of it never fire; it is once-only through `UPDATE batch_submissions SET follow_on_at = now() WHERE id = :s AND follow_on_at IS NULL RETURNING id` (the marker column of Task 1), so the two reconciliation paths and a resumed `batch_poll` cannot request twice; with the bulk operation still `active` it calls `create_operation(kind="batch_submit", payload={"bulk_operation_id", "llm_tuple_id", "after_submission_id": s}, dedup_key=f"batch_submit:{bulk}:after:{s}", defer=...)` deferred with `lock="batch"` (a key that can never collide with another submission's); a follow-on that finds the singleton occupied ends `NotPrepared("in_flight")` without consuming anything.
    - The `batch_submit` handler (`register_operation_handler("batch_submit")`), payload `{"bulk_operation_id", "llm_tuple_id"}`: in order, `recover_lost` for its own operation (a `preparing` row of a lost execution abandoned, a `submit_started` one made ambiguous, never resent); the four sweeps for the tuple (L3939 "the same sweeps as online admission", with Deviation 7's exhaustion sweep); `prepare` (a `NotPrepared` ends the operation `succeeded` with `progress = {"skipped": reason}`); `afault_point("batch.after_prepare_commit")`, then `submit_start` in its own transaction (its re-check abandons instead when the tuple, the bulk operation or a cancel request changed meanwhile); `BatchesClient.create` outside any transaction; `record_created | record_rejected | record_ambiguous` in its own transaction; `finish_in(succeeded)` (the submission's state is the outcome). The global purge fence refuses the submit before `submit_start` (L6365). The handler builds its `BatchesClient` from today's `OperationDeps` fields (`cfg`, `gateway.http_client`, `clock`); Task 27 moves the client into `OperationDeps.batches_client`.
  - `tests/payload/test_batch_params.py` (L3932): the batch params of an item equal the online generator body for the same prompt except `stream`, with `cache_control` on `system[1]` and `max_tokens` = the first stage.

the last case is `test_a_target_with_an_online_draft_is_resumed_not_batched` (Codex r4 B9), which also runs the next cycle and asserts `load_context` resumes that draft with no second `generate` call); `test_eligibility_is_decided_per_candidate` (Codex r5 B1): one candidate set of two fresh targets and one with an online draft under the active policy → `prepare` selects exactly the two fresh ones (the correlated fragment, bound with `:policy` only; a `:t` bind is never asked for); the order; the cut to `batch_max_items`, to the byte cap and to the allocation (`fits` with the §21.4 figures at the measured overhead: about 344 items of a $37.24 allocation); below `batch_min_targets` nothing is prepared and the targets stay `pending`; `test_eight_concurrent_sessions_with_a_batch_preparation_never_exceed_the_cap` (L5709: scoring, profile extraction, a batch prepare and a cap edit); `test_below_minimum_after_the_cas_rolls_everything_back` (Codex r2 B9): with `batch_min_targets = 10`, ten candidates of which one (and, in a second case, all ten) are claimed before the CAS: no submission, attempt, item or ledger row exists afterwards and the operation ends `succeeded` with `skipped = below_minimum`; `test_a_competing_funded_reservation_waits_and_sees_the_new_allocation` (Codex r2 B10, r3 B19): the prepare holds the month and bulk rows from its sizing to its reservations, so a funded follow-up reservation started meanwhile waits (`lock_wait_details`) and, once the prepare committed, is admitted or denied against the updated `spent_usd` and `followup_held_usd`; `test_an_insufficient_allocation_before_the_prepare_is_not_prepared`: with the allocation already short, `prepare` ends `NotPrepared("no_allocation")` and `reserve_batch`'s own check (a `Bug` if it ever fires after the sizing under the same locks) is never reached;   - `async batches.poll(engine, client, *, now, epoch, defer) -> PollReport`, the body of the `batch_poll` task (L3958): for each `submitted` submission (and each `ended` one not yet reconciled whose cancel request is pending): a pending cancel (`cancel_requested_at IS NOT NULL`, a `provider_batch_id` known and the batch not yet ended) → `client.cancel` (3 tries, then logged, L6443), then `retrieve`; `processing_status = 'ended'` → `submitted → ended` (`ended_at`); for each `ended` submission: stream `results()`, `process_item` per line in its own transaction, and when the stream was read to its end (`complete`) and every line's `custom_id` belongs to the submission, the **one completion orchestration** `batches.finalize_results(engine, submission_id, *, now, defer) -> Finalized(missing: int, reconciled: bool)` (`defer` is the calling task's deferrer, which `follow_on` needs; both callers, the poll and the adoption, pass theirs: Codex r7 B1), shared with the adoption path of `reconcile_ambiguous` (Codex r5 B5), **three kinds of transactions, never one** (Codex r6 B1: `process_item` takes the item's job row before the submission and ends on `ranking_state`, so a second item inside the same transaction would take a job row after `ranking_state` and could deadlock against a merge that holds that job and waits for the ranking row): (1) **completion**, one transaction — `active_config FOR SHARE` → the owner (`state = 'ended' AND results_complete_at IS NULL`) → `results_complete_at = now` (the column the `reconciled` CHECK of L2543 demands; the durable record that the stream was read to its end and every line was ours), `missing` = the count of items still `awaiting`, and `batch.items_missing` (critical, subject the submission id) when it is positive; a marker already set → nothing written; (2) **the missing items**, each in **its own transaction** through the **same** `process_item` with a synthetic `ItemResult(custom_id, result_type="missing", message=None)` (Codex r2 B14), in the schedule's order as for any item: its entry `dispatched → ambiguous` (A1 issue 22), then the target predicate (a superseded or cancelled target → `ignored_fenced`, as for any late item), the decision `retry_online` → `pending('batch_missing')`, the attempt still `running` → `transient_error`, the hold released once, the ranking bumped; (3) **reconcile**, one transaction — `active_config FOR SHARE` → the owner (`state = 'ended' AND results_complete_at IS NOT NULL`) → `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM batch_items WHERE submission_id = :s AND processed_at IS NULL)` → `ended → reconciled` (`reconciled_at`), `bulk.complete_if_done`, `follow_on(conn, submission_id=…, defer=defer)` **in that same transaction** (the once-only marker and the follow-on operation's insert commit with the reconcile, so a crash can neither lose nor double the request; its own rule decides whether it fires); an item still unprocessed leaves the submission `ended`. **Recovery between the steps is the rows** (every step is idempotent, and `batch_poll` runs every five minutes): an `ended` submission with `results_complete_at` NULL is streamed again (idempotent per item on `processed_at`) and then completed; one with `results_complete_at` set skips the stream and runs steps (2) and (3) — a crash after the completion commit processes the remaining `awaiting` items as `missing` on the next pass, a crash after the last missing item reconciles on the next pass; a cut stream never sets the marker, so no item is decided `missing` before the stream was read whole (L3961), and C23's idempotency holds throughout; at least one `followup` decision → `cycles.request_cycle(trigger="batch_followup")`. A `submitted` submission past `expires_at + 1 h` without `ended` is retrieved like any other (the provider ends it with expired items).
  - `async batches.process_item(conn, *, submission, item, result: ItemResult, now) -> Decision`: idempotent on `batch_items.processed_at` (a second call returns the stored decision); the locks in the schedule's order (Codex r1 #18): `active_config FOR SHARE`, the item's job row `FOR SHARE`, the owner `BatchSubmissionOwner(submission.id, submission.state)`, then the month row inside `settle`, then the target and the drafts, `ranking_state` last; **spend first, with the gateway's own classification** (Codex r1 #26): `succeeded` → `gateway.classify_message(node_model=<the item's model>, schema=<its generator schema>, message=<the result's message JSON>) -> Classified(code: str | None, stop_reason, model_served, refusal_category, parsed: GeneratorWire | None, usage)`, the function factored out of `_response` (`gateway.py:977–996`: `model_served ≠ requested` → `model_mismatch`; `max_tokens`; `refusal`; `end_turn` → the schema mirror, `parsing_error` and `storable` → `invalid_output` when any fails; else `unexpected_stop_reason`), then `settle(outcome="billed", usage=classified.usage, call=CallOutcome(status="completed", model_served, stop_reason, error_code=classified.code, refusal_category, output_json=<the wire JSON only when code is None>, provider_request_id=<message id>))` at batch prices (the ledger row's `batch` flag): the usage is **always** billed, a partial, refused, malformed or unstorable output is never stored, and a storable failure cannot roll the settlement back; `errored | canceled | expired` → `not_billed(batch_item_errored | batch_item_canceled | batch_item_expired)` with the call `error`; a `released` or already-`ambiguous` entry takes the late edges `ambiguous → billed | not_billed` (L2974–2975, Codex N9). **Then the decision**: the target `FOR UPDATE` with `state = 'in_batch' AND batch_submission_id = :s AND attempt_no = :a` (L3959) → otherwise `ignored_fenced` (the attempt, if still `running`, ends `cancelled('ignored_fenced')`; A2 B1: a cancel requested for a superseded tuple finds its targets already cancelled, so `canceled` items of that submission are `ignored_fenced`, Deviation 27). With the fence passed, the table of L3962–3976 verbatim, keyed by `classified.code`: `model_mismatch` → `failed_request('model_mismatch')` with the critical alert of G11 (the attempt `failed_request`; nothing is published under the wrong identity); `invalid_output` (a structural failure, a parse error or an unstorable output) → `retry_online` → `pending('batch_invalid_output')` with no draft and the attempt `failed_invalid_output('batch_invalid_output')`; `None` (`end_turn`, valid) → `parse_generator(classified.parsed, family=version.parser_family)`'s `ParsedGenerator` (the tuple's `PromptVersion`, loaded by `process_item` as `load_context` loads it, Task 4; Codex r5 B6) → `score_drafts(round = 0, validation = {"normalisation": …})` under the batch attempt, `validate_step` (the §9.4 `validate` logic: V1–V6 with the normalisation merged, the corrected draft written back, knockouts, the provisional aggregation under the active scope, T1–T8 under the **batch attempt's** policy, L3967; A2 R6: the follow-up attempt re-validates under its own policy at claim, so a policy change between them may publish without a critic, as §11.11 allows) → no trigger and no failure → `publish_result(mode="accepted", predicate=<batch>)` (`published`, the attempt `published_accepted`) else `followup`: `in_batch → pending` with `lane = 'batch_followup'`, `resume_draft_id`, `batch_submission_id` cleared, the attempt `batch_draft_ready`; `refused` → `refused(category)` with `refusal_category`, the attempt `refused`; `max_tokens` → `retry_online` → `pending('batch_max_tokens')` (the attempt `failed_max_tokens`; Task 9's `load_context` starts the next attempt enlarged); `unexpected_stop_reason` → `failed_invalid_output('unexpected_stop_reason')`; `errored`/`invalid_request` → `failed_request('batch_invalid_request')` (the attempt `failed_request`); `errored`/other → `retry_online` → `pending('batch_errored')` (the attempt `transient_error`); `canceled` with `cancel_requested_at` set → the fence (above), otherwise `retry_online` → `pending('batch_canceled')` (the attempt `transient_error`); `expired` → `retry_online` → `pending('batch_expired')` (the attempt `transient_error`). Every `retry_online` target is never batched again (`ELIGIBLE_SQL`, Task 16). Each decision writes `batch_items (result_type, stop_reason, decision, processed_at)` and, for every decision but `followup`, `bulk.release_item_hold(conn, item_id=item.id)` (Task 10; A2 B3/B4, Deviation 27; Codex r1 #27): the once-only release `UPDATE batch_items SET hold_released_at = now() WHERE id = :i AND hold_released_at IS NULL RETURNING hold_usd`, whose returned amount it subtracts from `followup_held_usd` (never below 0); a `followup` keeps its hold until its target reaches a terminal state (Task 10's `publish`/`fail`, a cancellation, an expiry, the exhaustion sweep, a merge or target-set deletion release it through Task 10's `bulk.release_target_hold`, keyed on the target's unreleased `followup` item; this task changes none of them), and `bulk.complete_if_done` (Task 14) releases whatever is left at completion.
  - `async batches.reconcile_ambiguous(engine, client, *, now, epoch, defer) -> ReconcileReport`, the body of `batch_reconcile_ambiguous` (L3984–3999; the task passes its deferrer, which the adoption's `finalize_results` hands to `follow_on`: Codex r7 B1), for each `submit_ambiguous` submission and each `released` one with `submit_started_at > now − 29 days` (A2 B12): `client.list_all()`; a listing error leaves every row untouched and logged (`batch_listing_failed`); the window `created_at ∈ [submit_started_at − 1 min, submit_started_at + 10 min]`; candidates = the window's batches whose `request_counts` total equals `request_count`; for each candidate that has `ended`: download its results completely; the `custom_id` set equal to ours → **confirmed**: `provider_batch_id` adopted, `submit_ambiguous | released → ended` (`ended_at`), a pending `cancel_requested_at` carried forward as a record only (a confirmed candidate has ended, so there is nothing to cancel: the request already cancelled the targets, and every item of the adopted batch is `ignored_fenced`; Codex r1 #24, r7 B3), the items processed per `process_item`, each in its own transaction after the adoption transaction committed (for a released submission every item is `ignored_fenced` and only its spend settles, L3999), then **the same `finalize_results` as the poll**: its completion step writes `results_complete_at` (the download was complete and the `custom_id` sets equal, so no item is `missing` here — an item of ours without a line is a partial intersection, quarantined by rule 4 and never adopted; Codex r6 B2) and its reconcile step moves `ended → reconciled` once every item is processed, in the adoption pass itself (the adoption never relies on a later poll to finish what it started: Codex r5 B5), with `follow_on(conn, submission_id=…, defer=defer)` inside that reconcile transaction (Codex r7 B1), whose rule fires for a `submit_ambiguous` origin and never for a released one, however the row is reached (the adoption path or a resumed `batch_poll` after a crash; Codex r2 B15); a partial intersection → `batch.custom_id_anomaly` (critical, subject the submission id) and the submission stays; otherwise `last_reconcile_at = now` and `reconcile_report = {"candidates": n, "listed": m}`; a `submit_ambiguous` submission with `submit_started_at < now − 25 h` → `open_alert("batch.submit_ambiguous_unresolved", critical, subject, context={"reserved_usd": …})` (never a transition, chair decision C3); a `released` submission past 29 days is listed no more. The workspace is assumed to be this installation's alone (A2 B15, Deviation 27).
  - `async batches.release(conn, submission_id, *, now, defer) -> Released(reserved_usd: Decimal)` (L3991–3997), in schedule order (the route holds `active_config FOR SHARE` and the idempotency row; Codex r2 B7): the submission row `FOR UPDATE` under `state = 'submit_ambiguous'` (the owner) → `submit_ambiguous → released` (`released_at`) → the month row and every item entry `dispatched → ambiguous` (`settle(outcome="ambiguous", call=CallOutcome(status="ambiguous"))`), counted at its reservation → the bulk row, the item holds released once (`bulk.release_item_hold` per item) → the attempts still `running` → `released` (a superseded one keeps `cancelled`; Codex r2 B13) → the targets `in_batch → pending` (owner `batch_submission_id = :s AND attempt_no = :a`; cancelled ones untouched) → `batch.submit_ambiguous_unresolved` resolved → `follow_on(conn, submission_id=…, defer=defer)` (fires for the release; the route's deferrer, as `score_now` passes its own; Codex r7 B1) → `ranking.bump` last. The route is Task 21's (`POST /api/batches/{id}/release`).
  - `batches.quarantined_usd(conn) -> Decimal`: Σ `reserved_usd` of `submit_ambiguous` submissions (S:L1199), by executing Task 10's `bulk.QUARANTINED_USD_SQL`, the one statement `fail`'s `llm.cap_reached` context already uses (`/api/system/spend`).

### Task 18: The remaining reconcilers, the alerts, the scoring metrics and the health section

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/reconcilers.py` (`stuck_targets`, `det_completeness`, `admission_alerts`, `scoring_drift`), `tests/integration/test_stuck_targets.py`, `tests/integration/test_det_completeness.py`, `tests/integration/test_scoring_alerts.py`, `tests/unit/test_scoring_metrics.py`, `tests/integration/test_lock_schedule.py` (Deviation 38)
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/observability/metrics.py`, `observability/health.py`, `llm/spend_ledger.py` (`budget_counters` covers bulk `spent_usd`), `scoring/cycles.py` (`critic_rate_7d`), `graphs/score/nodes/finalize_cycle.py` (the `scoring.critic_rate_high` evaluation, which Task 11 left to this task), `tests/unit/test_worker_main.py` (the registration pin updated to what this task's modules register: `stuck_targets`, `det_completeness`, `admission_alerts`, `scoring_drift`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `reconciler`, `fence_epoch`, `assert_fence_rule`; `transition_where`; `det_results.compute_and_write`, `incomplete_results`; `raise_alert`, `open_alert`, `resolve_alert`, `resolve_alerts`; the `score_cycles.report` of Task 11; `EvaluationPolicy`, `projected_critic_rate`; `Gauge`, `Counter` (`observability/metrics.py`); `system_health` (`observability/health.py:500`).
- Produces:
  - `@reconciler("stuck_targets", "tick")` (L5504, §7.17 #3): `in_progress` targets with `claim_heartbeat_at < now − 3 min` whose attempt is still `running` (a target whose attempt a fenced publish already ended `cancelled` is left for the purge's chunk; A2 S4, Deviation 28) → `pending` while `charged_attempts < 4`, else `failed_attempts_exhausted('stuck')`, the attempts `claim_lost`, through `transition_where`; `jobhunter_claim_reclaims_total` incremented after the commit; `scoring.stuck_target_repaired` (warn, subject the hour `now.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H")`, L5594 "aggregated hourly") raised per pass with reclaims and resolved by a pass that finds the previous hour's subject with no reclaim in the last hour. The takeover sweep of Task 11 shares the statements.
  - `@reconciler("det_completeness", "heavy")` (L5511, §7.17 #5): every published result of the active tuple without a current instance for the active scope (and the pending scope while one exists) whose hash and version are current → `compute_and_write`, in chunks of 500 under `active_config FOR SHARE`; a changed `DET_ALGORITHM_VERSION` therefore recomputes every current instance (L4568).
  - `@reconciler("admission_alerts", "heavy")` (§9.6.5, L3686–3688; A2 A8, Deviation 28): `admission.exploration_hits` (warn): over the trailing 14 days, with ≥ 10 published targets whose publishing attempt has `score_attempts.lane = 'exploration'` (the lane snapshot of Task 1; Codex r3 B15), more than 25% with the current instance's `score_mid ≥ relevance_threshold`; resolved below the threshold; `admission.high_sim_expired` (warn): ≥ 3 targets that entered `expired_unscored` in the last 24 h with `similarity ≥` the 70th percentile of `similarity` over the targets with an attempt of `score_attempts.lane = 'similarity'` started in the trailing 7 days (never the mutable `score_targets.lane`, which a retry overwrites); resolved after 24 h without recurrence; `admission.backlog_drain` (info): `count(pending of the active tuple) / (admitted in the trailing 7 days / 7) > max_ad_age_days`, raised at most once a day (subject the UTC date).
  - `@reconciler("scoring_drift", "heavy")` (§11.13, L4633–4635): `scoring.feedback_disagreement` (warn): over 14 days with ≥ 10 `score_feedback` rows, more than 30% are `poor` on snapshots with `M ≥ τ` or `good` with `M < τ − 20`; `scoring.distribution_shift` (info): the 7-day mean `score_mid` of the active tuple's current instances differs by more than 10 from the previous 28 days' mean under the same tuple; `scoring.critic_rate_high` (warn, L5595): `critic_rate_7d` = first-round critic calls ÷ attempts with ≥ 1 dispatched call over the trailing 7 days, n ≥ 50 attempts, `> 1.5 × projected_critic_rate` of the active policy; evaluated here and at every `finalize_cycle`.
  - `cycles.critic_rate_7d(conn, *, llm_tuple_id, evaluation_policy_id, now) -> tuple[Decimal | None, int]` (the rate and `n`), also used by `/api/system/spend` (Task 21).
  - `budget_counters` (M2, heavy) also recomputes each active bulk operation's `spent_usd` from the ledger (reserved, dispatched and ambiguous entries at `reserved_usd`, billed at `settled_usd`, not_billed at 0, for entries with that `bulk_operation_id`) and corrects it under the row lock with `llm.budget_counter_drift` (§7.17 #7's first clause; A1 issue 29, Deviation 28).
  - Metrics (§17.5 L5538–5539): `jobhunter_critic_rate_7d` (gauge), `jobhunter_critic_verdict_mismatch_total` (counter, defined and incremented by Task 9; pinned here), `jobhunter_score_targets{state}` (gauge per state), `jobhunter_oldest_pending_target_age_seconds` (gauge), `jobhunter_claim_reclaims_total` (counter); `metrics_refresh` (M1, heavy) sets the three gauges.

  - `llm.auth_failed`'s pause ends with an acknowledgement as well as with a billed try (`pause_reason` reads `acknowledged_at`; B R11, Deviation 19): a paused lane makes no scoring try, and the non-scoring calls (a profile extraction, a dry run) resolve it when they succeed.

A NUL or lone surrogate in `comment` is 422 at the route.
  - `golden.list_cases(conn, *, after_id: int | None, limit: int, active: bool | None) -> list[CaseRow(id, job_id, label, comment, profile_version, scope_version, source, active, is_canary)]` (keyset by id); `golden.delete_case(conn, case_id) -> bool` (the row and, by cascade, its `eval_case_results` and eval calls; no deletion manifest yet: §14.2 L4979's `golden_case_delete` manifest is M5's, Deviation 21; the job's inputs lose their exemption at once); `golden.set_flags(conn, case_id, *, active: bool | None, is_canary: bool | None) -> CaseRow` (R2's `PATCH`; at most `MAX_CANARIES = 2` cases may be canaries, L4632: a third answers 422).

  - `golden.seed_adversarial(conn, *, fixtures_dir: Path, now) -> Seeded(cases: int, jobs: int, profiles: int)`, idempotent by the fixture's `key`: for each fixture, a synthetic `cv_files` row (the fixture's profile text as `stripped_text`, a synthetic `strip_report`), a `profile_drafts` row in `confirmed`, a `profile_versions` row with `synthetic_eval = true` and the fixture's profile JSON (one profile per fixture, L4610 "each with its own profile and scope"), a `scope_versions` row by `ensure_hashed` of the fixture's scope, a `jobs` row with `synthetic_eval = true` (`availability = 'active'`, `company_key = 'eval-' + key`, the fixture's structured fields and det facts) and one `job_revisions` row (the fixture's description) as its current revision, and the `golden_cases` row (`source = 'adversarial'`, `label`, `expected_knockouts` from the fixture, `det_inputs_snapshot` built with `det_inputs` from the fixture's facts, `active = true`). Synthetic jobs get no posting, no target (`create_targets_for_tuple`, node 9 and `missing_targets` select `NOT synthetic_eval`), no embedding (the `awaiting_embedding` scan excludes them, Files) and no row in `current_scores` or `display_scores` (the views' `NOT j.synthetic_eval`, Task 1; Codex r1 #32). `jobhunter golden seed-adversarial [--fixtures <dir>]` runs it (default the packaged `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/eval_fixtures/adversarial/`, found through `jobhunter.scoring.eval_fixtures`, so the dev stack's installed wheel has them).
  - `eval_runner.run_eval(engine, gateway, *, eval_run_id: int, op: OperationContext, now) -> EvalOutcome`, the `eval_run` operation handler's body (`register_operation_handler("eval_run")`, payload `{"eval_run_id"}`, dedup key `eval_run:<id>`; the `eval_runs` row and the operation are inserted in one transaction by the CLI and by `canary_daily`): reads the run's candidate (`rubric_version_id`, `prompt_schema_version_id` → `load_prompt_version`, `model_policy_id`, `evaluation_policy_id`, `execution_config_id`, `route`, `repetitions`); the handler builds the score steps' dependencies from today's `OperationDeps` fields (`cfg`, `gateway`, `clock`; `worker/operations.py:134–147`), and Task 27 moves them into `OperationDeps.score_steps`; **the owner is the operation's claim** (Codex r1 #29): every paid call carries `CallContext(owner=EvalRunOwner(eval_run_id, claim=op.claim), purpose_class="nonscoring", execution_config_id, eval_run_id, golden_case_id, round, logical_call_no, deadline)` (with `input_refs["repetition"]`, for the ledger's audit only; no step key, nothing is ever replayed from a receipt), and every domain write (a case row, the run's completion) runs under `lock_operation_owner(conn, op.claim)` then the `eval_runs` row `FOR SHARE` while `running`, so a reclaimed execution can neither reserve nor write (`FencedOut`); the lock order inside a case is `active_config FOR SHARE` → the case's job row `FOR SHARE` → the operation row → the eval run row → month → ledger → `eval_case_results`. **One execution, no resume** (round-4 ruling (a), replacing the receipt replay of r1 #30, r2 B19, r3 B4, B5; Codex r4 B4–B7; Deviation 29): an eval run is scored by **one execution** from its first call to its completion. The steps of a case run over the in-memory `MemoryDraftStore` (Task 9's `DraftStore` protocol: the drafts, validations and reviews of the case live in the handler's memory, nothing is written between calls), the routing is exactly the production `routing.route` of Task 9 on the gateway outcomes the execution itself received (so an exhausted critic repair or a final-round `revise | incomplete` publishes contested, L3557–3558, L3561, as online), and the case's **only** durable write is its terminal row, `eval_case_results(outcome ∈ accepted | contested | refused | failed | filtered_out, critic_ran, llm_call_ids, output = <the publication object plus the rounds' triggers and derived outcomes>, score_mid)`, inserted in one transaction after the case's last call returned. There is no step store, no step key, no receipt lookup and no replay: `eval_case_results.outcome` keeps the spec's five values (Task 1). **An interrupted run is failed, never resumed**: the handler's first transaction, under `lock_operation_owner` and the run row `FOR UPDATE`, first leaves a run that is already `completed` or `failed` as it is and ends the operation `succeeded` through `op.finish_in(conn, status="succeeded")` in that transaction (the state a crash between the run's terminal commit and the operation's end leaves, Codex r5 B2), and otherwise asks whether an earlier execution has already touched the run — `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM llm_calls WHERE eval_run_id = :r) OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM eval_case_results WHERE eval_run_id = :r)` (the gateway settles inside `call()` before it returns, `gateway.py:999–1002`, so every dispatched call of the run has a row) — and, when it has, ends the run `running → failed` with `set_={"metrics": metrics || {"failed": "interrupted"}, "cost_usd": <Σ settled calls of the run; ambiguous at reserved>}` and ends the operation `succeeded` in the **same** transaction through `op.finish_in(conn, status="succeeded")` (`worker/operations.py:182–194`: the handler-side end that commits with the caller's last domain write, so the run's terminal state and its operation's end are never observed apart; the operation did its work: the run's state is final); an execution that crashed before its first call left nothing, so the reclaimed handler is simply the run's first execution. The old execution, if it still runs, is `FencedOut` at its next reservation and at its case write (the owner is the claim); a call of it that returns afterwards is settled by the gateway as every call is, and the ledger, never the run row, is the authority for spend (`cost_usd` is informational and taken at the fail). The user starts a new run (`jobhunter eval run` again, or the next `canary_daily`) which scores every case afresh: the cost of an interruption is one run (about $2.70 for a golden run at the §21 figures), and in exchange the recovery has no state machine, no receipt classes and no parity question with the production route. Per case: the job `FOR SHARE` and `is_fenced(job_id)` → skipped and reported `fenced`, no row (L6362); the case's hard filters under the case scope from the frozen snapshot (`det_facts_from_inputs`) → `eval_case_results(outcome = 'filtered_out', critic_ran = false, llm_call_ids = [], output = {"filters": …})`; otherwise the production route with the steps of Task 9, the candidate's rubric and prompt-schema version rendered as for a target (every call carries `golden_case_id`, L4611; the ledger books it non-scoring under the reserve, L2341), the triggers under the candidate policy with the case's T8 key, `route = 'forced_critic'` forcing one critic round (L4612), the publication object through Task 10's **pure** `publication.build` (the contested null-out, the repairs, the strict model, the V6 guard and the aggregation under the case scope from the snapshot; no target, result, det instance or ranking row is written: Codex r2 B18); a gateway failure of a case is `failed` and the run goes on. **Repetitions, deterministic and adaptive** (§11.13 L4613 "runs 10 cases twice"; Codex r2 B22): a `golden` run first scores every active case once (repetition 1); then it attempts repetition 2 in **case-id order** over the active cases, skipping a case whose repetition 1 has no numeric `score_mid`, until ten cases have two numeric results or `MAX_RETEST_TRIES = 20` second repetitions were attempted or the cases are exhausted (the call bound: at most 20 extra cases); the execution's own list of results is the progress (there is no resume); `repetitions = 2` on the row; `canary` runs once. Task 20's sample is therefore exactly the cases that ended with two numeric results, in id order. The run ends `completed` with `metrics = {"transport", "transport_requested", "cases", "repetitions"}` and `passed = NULL` (`eval_runs.passed` is nullable, S:L2633; Task 20 adds `calibration.metrics` and `calibration.gate` to this terminal transaction), `cost_usd` = Σ settled (ambiguous at reserved), **and its operation ends `succeeded` in the same transaction** through `op.finish_in(conn, status="succeeded")` after the run's transition (owner → run row, steps 5 and 10; `operations.py:182–194`), never through the framework's separate finish: a crash before that commit leaves the run `running` and touched (the next execution fails it `interrupted`), a crash after it leaves nothing to do (Codex r5 B2); fault points `eval.before_terminal_commit` and `eval.after_terminal_commit`. **Abandonment** (Codex r1 #31): `_fail_abandoned_eval_runs` fails a `running` run only when it has no live `eval_run` operation (`operations.kind = 'eval_run' AND (payload ->> 'eval_run_id')::bigint = e.id AND status IN ('pending', 'running')`) and, when no such operation ever existed (a CLI schema probe), `started_at < now − EVAL_RUN_ABANDONED`; an operation-backed run's health is its operation's claim and heartbeat (the `operations` reconciler), and the `eval_run` failure hook (written here, in `worker/operations.py`; Task 27 pins it) fails the run with `metrics.failed = "interrupted"` when its operation ends `failed` **while the run is still `running`** (`transition_where(["running"], "failed", …)`: a run already `completed` or `failed` is left as it is; the hook writes the run row after the operation row, steps 5 and 10 of the schedule, and needs no earlier lock; Codex r5 B2); a healthy run may take hours. `jobhunter eval run --kind golden (--candidate active|<rubric>,<prompt_schema>,<model_policy>,<evaluation_policy>,<execution_config> | --candidate-payload <settings.json>) [--route forced_critic] (--live | --fake)` inserts the `eval_runs` row and the operation in one transaction and waits for it, against the installation's database (the golden set lives there; the manual workflow of Task 20 is the adversarial smoke). **Transport provenance** (Codex r2 B20): the row records `metrics = {"transport_requested": "live" | "fake"}` at creation; the handler's first transaction compares it with the worker's mode (`JOBHUNTER_FAKE_LLM`, the flag the gateway's transport was built from) and, when they differ, ends the run `running → failed` with `set_={"metrics": metrics || {"failed": "transport_mismatch"}}` before any dispatch (`eval_runs` has no reason column and its registry entry has `reason_col=None`, `db/transitions.py:429–437`; `metrics` is settable: Codex r3 B6), so a fake run on a live worker pays nothing and a live run on a fake worker records nothing; the run ends with `metrics.transport` set to the transport that actually served it, exactly as the dry-run does. **A candidate that does not exist yet** (Codex r2 B21, r3 B7): `--candidate-payload` validates the file as the settings dry-run does and inserts its derived rows through `settings.derive.ensure_derived` in a short transaction that holds `active_config FOR UPDATE` — the lock `ensure_derived` relies on to serialise `evaluation_policies.version_no = max + 1` (`derive.py:90–91`, `seed.py:212–218`; two unserialised stagings, or a staging and a save, would race for one `version_no` and the loser would find no row) — content-addressed by hash, no settings version, no activation, no change to `active_config`'s columns; then it runs against those ids; the later save finds the same rows by hash, so the gate's run is the candidate's.

`test_a_reclaimed_eval_execution_can_neither_pay_nor_write` (Codex r1 #29): a `PausePoint` inside a case, the operation reclaimed by a second epoch, the old execution's next reservation and its case write both `FencedOut`, the new execution ends the run `failed` with `metrics.failed = "interrupted"` and its operation `succeeded`, and the ledger holds every call the old execution settled; a failure hook run against a `completed` run leaves it `completed`; `test_transport_mismatch_fails_before_any_call` (Codex r2 B20, r3 B6): a run requested `fake` on a live-configured worker, and one requested `live` on the fake, each end `failed` with `metrics.failed = "transport_mismatch"` and `fake_llm.served == 0`; - Consumes: `eval_runs`, `eval_case_results`, `golden_cases` (Task 1); `run_eval` and its terminal transaction (which ends a run `completed` with `metrics = {"transport", "transport_requested", "cases", "repetitions"}` and `passed = NULL`), `golden.tuple_identity` (Task 19); `validate.schema_verified` (Task 11); `GateState`, `eval_gate_state`, `warnings_only`, `_check_eval_gate`, `_GATED` (`settings/validate.py:59–323`, `settings/save.py:192–204`); `ensure_evaluation_policy` (`db/seed.py:211–227`), `DEFAULT_PROJECTED_CRITIC_RATE`; `score_drafts.provisional`, `.validation`, `.triggers`; `raise_alert`.
- Produces:
  - `calibration.Metrics` (frozen dataclass) and `calibration.metrics(results: Sequence[CaseResult], *, cases: Mapping[int, CaseRow], projected_critic_rate: Decimal, central_cost_usd: Decimal, similarity_cutoff: Decimal | None) -> Metrics`, pure, with `CaseResult(golden_case_id, repetition, outcome, critic_ran, score_mid, nulled_criteria, grounding_ok: bool, schema_ok: bool, knockouts_applied: list[str], hard_filtered: bool, cost_usd)`; judged with `ordinal(good) = 2, ok = 1, poor = 0` on the production route (L4614). **Three observation sets, declared per metric** (Codex r4 B10): the **primary** set is one observation per active case, its `repetition = 1` row (`calibration.primary(results)`), and every gate metric below is computed over it, so which cases got a second repetition can change no gate; the **paired** set is the test–retest sample (both repetitions of the cases that have two numeric results), used by `test_retest_sd` only; the **cost** set is every row, used by `mean_cost_usd` only. `schema_validity` = the share of primary results that are `accepted | contested | refused | filtered_out` (a `failed` outcome is a schema or transport failure); `grounding_rate` = the share of published accepted ratings whose every evidence quote grounded; `spearman_rho` over `(score_mid, ordinal)` of published cases with average ranks for ties (A3 G20, Deviation 29); `pairwise_agreement` = the share of case pairs with different labels whose `score_mid` order matches the label order; `false_exclusions` = `good` cases that were hard-filtered or carry a binding hard knockout; `knockout_recall` = the share of `expected_knockouts` that appear in the case's applied binding knockouts (cases without expectations are excluded; A3 G21: vacuous for week-1 cases); `contested_rate` over published cases; `test_retest_sd` over the test–retest sample (`calibration.test_retest_sample(results) -> list[int]`: the cases that **have** two numeric `score_mid` results, in id order, at most ten; which cases get a second repetition is the runner's adaptive procedure of Task 19, so the sample is read from the results, never inferred from a schedule that did not run: Codex r2 B22) = the mean of the per-case SD of `score_mid` across the two repetitions, and `None` when fewer than **five** such cases exist (missing evidence, never a pass; Codex r1 #33); `per_profile` for every profile with ≥ 10 cases (`rho`, `contested_rate`, `below_pooled: bool`); reported only: `critic_rate` vs `projected_critic_rate`, `admission_recall` (the share of `good` cases whose target similarity reaches `similarity_cutoff`, `None` without one), `mean_cost_usd` vs `central_cost_usd`.
  - `run_eval`'s terminal transaction (Task 19) gains the calibration: before the run's `running → completed`, it computes `calibration.metrics(...)` over the run's case results, merges them into `metrics` (beside `transport`, `transport_requested`, `cases`, `repetitions`) and sets `passed` from `calibration.gate(...)`, in the same transaction as the operation's `finish_in`.
  - `calibration.gate(m: Metrics, *, active_cases: int) -> GateState`: `"uncalibrated"` while `active_cases < 30` (L4631); `"passed"` iff `schema_validity == 1`, `grounding_rate == 1`, `spearman_rho ≥ 0.6`, `pairwise_agreement ≥ 0.75`, `false_exclusions == 0`, `knockout_recall == 1`, `contested_rate ≤ 0.15` and `test_retest_sd is not None and test_retest_sd ≤ 5` (L4616–4625: the test–retest gate is measured, never waived; a run without the repeated sample is `"failed"` with `metrics.failed = ["test_retest_missing"]`; Codex r1 #33); else `"failed"`, with `metrics.failed` naming every gate that failed.
  - `validate.Candidate(rubric_version_id: int, prompt_schema_version_id: int, model_policy_hash: bytes, evaluation_policy_hash: bytes)` and `async validate.eval_gate_state(conn, *, candidate: Candidate | None = None) -> GateState` (B R14, Deviation 29): `"uncalibrated"` while fewer than 30 active golden cases exist; otherwise the newest `completed` `eval_runs` row with `kind = 'golden'`, `route = 'production'`, the candidate's four identities (the policy rows found by hash; the execution config is not part of the identity, L4631) and **live provenance** (`metrics ->> 'transport' = 'live'`; a `fake` run counts only when the process itself runs on the fake, `JOBHUNTER_FAKE_LLM`, the dev stack and the tests: Codex r2 B20) decides: `passed` → `"passed"`, `False` → `"failed"`, no such run → `"failed"` (a run is required). `dryrun.dry_run` and `save._check_eval_gate` build the candidate from the proposed payload (its model policy and evaluation blocks hashed by `content_hash`, the active rubric and prompt-schema pointers); the shipped-version activations of Task 21 pass theirs. `eval.gate_failed` (warn, L5618) is raised when a golden run of a candidate ends `passed = false`. `validate.schema_verified(conn, *, prompt_schema_version_id, model_policy_id)` (Task 11's: true iff live runs of the version hold a passed case for every (schema, model) pair the policy's five nodes need; a policy that moves any of the five nodes to an unverified model is refused, as the user's answer to question 4 requires: Codex r2 B24) is read by `cycles.pause_reason` (the online and batch admission guard, Task 11), by `_check_eval_gate`, which refuses an `llm_tuple` save whose policy is not verified for the active version with 409 `conflict_state` (`detail = "schema_dry_run_required"`), and by the prompt-schema activation of Task 21; the dry-run CLI takes `--model-policy <id>` or `--candidate-payload <settings.json>` to run the cases of a candidate policy: the payload form validates the file as the settings dry-run does and inserts the policy row through `ensure_derived` in a short transaction under `active_config FOR UPDATE` (the lock that serialises `evaluation_policies.version_no`, `derive.py:90–91`, `seed.py:212–218`; content-addressed, no settings version, no activation; Codex r3 B7), because the save creates that row only after `_check_eval_gate` (`save.py:334–339`, `derive.py:95`) and the proof must exist first (Codex r2 B21); the later save finds the same row by hash, so a refused save is unblocked by running the dry-run on the payload it was refused for.
  - `evaluation_policy.replay_projected_critic_rate(conn, policy: EvaluationPolicy, *, now) -> Decimal | None` (§11.7.4 L4480): over the round-0 `score_drafts` of the last 30 days (≥ 50 rows, else `None`), the share whose stored `provisional` and `validation` would trigger any of T1–T8 under the new thresholds (`triggers.evaluate` re-run with `critic_rounds = 0` on the stored figures); `ensure_evaluation_policy` stores it at the row's creation and `DEFAULT_PROJECTED_CRITIC_RATE` otherwise (M2 Deviation 16 closed). The rate is fixed at creation: an identical payload reuses its row and its rate (B R9, Deviation 29; L4480 "computed when a policy row is created").
  - `canary_daily` (periodic `41 6 * * *` on the `tick` queue, `queueing_lock = "canary_daily"`, L285): with a tuple active and at least one `is_canary` active case, `INSERT eval_runs (kind = 'canary', route = 'production', <the active rubric, prompt schema, model policy, evaluation policy and execution config>)` and `create_operation(kind="eval_run", payload={"eval_run_id"}, dedup_key=f"eval_run:{id}")` (the runner of Task 19 scores the canaries under their own profile and scope, L4632); `calibration.canary_drift(conn, *, eval_run_id) -> list[Drift]` after the run: for each canary, `|score_mid − baseline| > 8` against the **first** canary `score_mid` under the key `(golden_case_id, rubric_version_id, prompt_schema_version_id, model_policy_id, evaluation_policy_id)` (A1 issue 25, Deviation 29) → `scoring.canary_drift` (warn, subject the case id).
  - `.github/workflows/eval-manual.yml` (§18.6 L5818–5828) is the **adversarial smoke**, not the activation gate (Codex r1 #34; Deviation 29): `workflow_dispatch` with the inputs `route` (`production | forced_critic`) and `candidate` (`active` only: a throwaway database holds the shipped versions and nothing else, so no other id can resolve there); the dry-run workflow's throwaway PostgreSQL and secret files; `jobhunter migrate`, `jobhunter golden seed-adversarial`, then **a worker**, because the eval CLI inserts an operation and waits for it and nothing else would run it (unlike `schema-dry-run`, which calls the gateway itself; `.github/workflows/schema-dry-run-manual.yml:90–106` starts none; Codex r3 B13): `uv run jobhunter worker &` with the same secrets directory and `JOBHUNTER_FAKE_LLM` unset (live-configured), readiness awaited by polling `worker_heartbeat` through `psql` for up to 60 s, then `jobhunter eval run --live --candidate active …` over the ten adversarial cases with the repository secret `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_EVAL`, and in the `if: always()` cleanup `kill -TERM` of the worker and a wait for its exit before the secret files and the database go; the case table (label, outcome, `score_mid`, binding knockouts against `expected_knockouts`) printed and uploaded as a markdown artifact; the gate state reported, which is `uncalibrated` by construction (ten cases). The CLI refuses to dispatch when a candidate id does not resolve or when `--kind golden` finds no active case (`eval_no_cases`), before any paid call. The golden-set evaluation the gate needs (≥ 30 of the user's cases) runs on the installation, `jobhunter eval run --live --kind golden --candidate …` (a runbook entry of Task 27), and §18.6's "required before merging prompt, rubric or LLM-library changes" is read as: the adversarial smoke in CI, the golden run on the installation before activation (Task 25 writes the note). It never runs on push or pull request.

### Task 21: The M3 routes (R10): shipped-version activation, score-now, retry, the manual cycle, batch release, feedback, golden cases, alert acknowledgement, and the M3 fields of `/api/system/spend`

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/src/jobhunter/api/routes/{targets,golden,alerts}.py`, `backend/src/jobhunter/settings/activate.py` (the shipped-version activation), `tests/integration/test_api_activation.py`, `tests/integration/test_api_score_now.py`, `tests/integration/test_api_batches.py`, `tests/integration/test_api_golden.py`, `tests/integration/test_api_alerts.py`
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/api/routes/{settings,system,spend}.py`, `api/app.py` (the routers), `api/errors.py` (`ProblemCode` unchanged: `budget_exhausted`, `filtered_requires_force`, `eval_gate_failed`, `conflict_state` exist), `frontend/src/api/schema.d.ts` (regenerated), `backend/src/jobhunter/settings/save.py` (`save_settings` gains `forced_effects`, below), `tests/integration/test_spend_route.py`
- Test: as above, plus `tests/unit/test_openapi_m3.py` (the new paths are in the document)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `run_idempotent`, the `active_config FOR UPDATE` → `run_idempotent` pattern (`api/routes/settings.py:316–397`, `profile.py:485–570`); `save_settings(conn, req, *, now, defer, internal=False)`, `SaveRequest`, `ensure_tuple`, `_check_eval_gate` (Task 20's candidate form); `targets.score_now`, `retry` (Task 13); `cycles.request_cycle`, `pause_reason` (Task 11); `batches.release`, `quarantined_usd` (Task 17); `golden.record_feedback`, `list_cases`, `delete_case`, `set_flags` (Task 19); `alerts.acknowledge_alert` (Task 13); `cycles.c_hat`, `critic_rate_7d` (Tasks 11, 18); `api/pagination.py` (the signed cursor); `schema_dry_run` rows (Task 4).
- Produces (every POST below requires `Idempotency-Key` where §14.1 L4963 lists it: score-now, retry, activate, release, score-cycle):
  - `POST /api/settings/rubric-versions/{id}/activate` and `POST /api/settings/prompt-schema-versions/{id}/activate` `{expected_rescore_ack: bool, eval_override_ack?: bool}` → 201 `{version, llm_tuple_id, targets_to_create, bulk_budget_usd}` (L5005–5006; §13.3 L4900): `activate.activate_shipped_version(conn, *, kind, version_id, req, now, defer) -> SaveOutcome`, in this order (Codex r1 #35): 1. `active_config FOR UPDATE`; 2. `run_idempotent`; 3. the version row, else 404; 4. for the prompt-schema form, `validate.schema_verified(conn, prompt_schema_version_id=version_id, model_policy_id=<active>)` (Task 11: live, per version and per model; M2 Carry-forward 1; B R13: never "the latest run") → else 409 `conflict_state` with `detail = "schema_dry_run_required"`; 5. the eval gate for the candidate (the new pointer with the active others) → 409 `eval_gate_failed` without `eval_override_ack`; 6. `expected_rescore_ack` required while retained revisions exist (as the confirm, M2 Deviation 42) → else 422; 7. the pointer flip (`UPDATE active_config SET rubric_version_id | prompt_schema_version_id = :id`); 8. `save_settings(conn, SaveRequest(payload=<the active version's payload>, base_version_id=<the active version>, acknowledged_warnings=<the dry-run's warnings>, eval_override_ack=req.eval_override_ack), now=now, defer=defer, internal=True, forced_effects=frozenset({"llm_tuple"}))`, where `save_settings` gains `forced_effects: frozenset[EffectClass] = frozenset()`, merged into the derived `effects` after the dry-run (the real function derives effects from the payload, `settings/save.py:333`, so an unchanged payload would otherwise flip nothing): `_apply_llm_tuple` then runs `ensure_tuple` on the flipped pointers and records the new `llm_tuple_id` flip, the settings version row records the forced class in `effect_classes` and the override, `_FLIP` runs after every action (`:372–387`); **after `_FLIP`** `save_settings` calls `targets.activate_tuple` when `a.tuple_flipped` (Task 14: the bulk operation under the month row, the cancellations, the cancel requests on the submissions, `create_targets_for_tuple`), and the tuple flip's `ranking.bump` moves after the activation so `ranking_state` is the transaction's last lock (`… → _FLIP → activate_tuple → ranking.bump`; Codex r2 B7); the confirm path reaches the same `activate_tuple` after its step 7 and bumps the ranking after it too. The 201 body `{version: <version_no>, llm_tuple_id, targets_to_create, bulk_budget_usd}`.
  - `GET /api/settings/rubric-versions` → `[{id, version, content_hash, created_at, active}]` and `GET /api/settings/prompt-schema-versions` → `[{id, prompt_schema_hash, created_at, active, dry_run: {eval_run_id, passed, transport, finished_at} | null}]` (the newest dry-run of that version), so the id to activate is discoverable (Deviation 23; M4's "new rubric v2 available" reads them).
  - `POST /api/jobs/{job_id}/score-now` `{force?: bool}` → 202 `{target_id, state}`; 409 `budget_exhausted`, 409 `filtered_requires_force`, 409 `conflict_state`, 404 (L4976; `active_config FOR SHARE` then `run_idempotent` then `targets.score_now`). `POST /api/targets/{target_id}/retry` → 202 `{target_id, state}` (L4985). `POST /api/duplicate-groups/{group_id}/score-individually` stays M4's (M1 Carry-forward 3; the domain function exists).
  - `POST /api/system/score-cycle` → 202 `{queued: bool, reason: str | null}` (L5028; `cycles.request_cycle(trigger="manual")` after `active_config FOR SHARE` and `run_idempotent`; `queued` is false when a `manual` job already waits, `reason` is `pause_reason` or `"no_pending"` read without locks, so the user learns at once why nothing will happen; the cycle id is the worker's, visible through the health section's `running_cycle_id`; Deviation 39).
  - `POST /api/batches/{id}/release` `{confirm: true}` → 202 `{submission_id, state: "released", reserved_usd}` (L5027); 409 `conflict_state` unless the submission is `submit_ambiguous`; 422 without `confirm: true`.
  - `POST /api/jobs/{job_id}/feedback` `{kind: "label" | "wrong_score", label: "good" | "ok" | "poor", comment?: str (≤ 2,000)}` → 201 `{feedback_id, golden_case_id}` (L4977); 409 `conflict_state` without a published result. `GET /api/golden-cases?cursor&limit&active` → keyset `[{id, job_id, label, comment, profile_version, scope_version, source, active, is_canary}]` (L4978). `DELETE /api/golden-cases/{id}` → 204 (L4979; the manifest is M5's). `PATCH /api/golden-cases/{id}` `{active?: bool, is_canary?: bool}` → 200 the row (R2).
  - `POST /api/alerts/{id}/ack` → 200 `{id, acknowledged_at}` (L5030; R10: the score-lane pause of `llm.reservation_bound_violated` and `llm.auth_failed` is lifted by it); 404 for a resolved or unknown alert.
  - `GET /api/system/spend`: `c_hat` from `cycles.c_hat` (history or central, plus `c_hat_source`), `critic_rate_7d` from `cycles.critic_rate_7d` (`null` below 50 attempts), `bulk_operation: {id, state, budget_usd, spent_usd, followup_held_usd, llm_tuple_id} | null`, `quarantined_batch_usd` from `batches.quarantined_usd`, and `pause_reason`. `SpendOut.bulk_operation` becomes `BulkOperationOut | None` (the OpenAPI document changes; `scripts/check-openapi-drift.sh` and the regenerated `schema.d.ts` land in the same commit, B R18).

```python
# tests/integration/test_api_score_now.py  (Review Focus 5)
async def test_two_concurrent_score_now_clicks_give_one_override_and_one_cycle(logged_in_client, engine, pending_target) -> None:
    body = {"force": False}
    r1, r2 = await asyncio.gather(
        logged_in_client.post(f"/api/jobs/{job_id}/score-now", json=body, headers=idem("tab-1")),
        logged_in_client.post(f"/api/jobs/{job_id}/score-now", json=body, headers=idem("tab-2")),
    )
    assert {r1.status_code, r2.status_code} == {202} and r1.json()["target_id"] == r2.json()["target_id"]
    row = await target_row(engine, pending_target)
    assert (row.override, row.lane, row.state) == ("score_now", "score_now", "pending") and row.override_at is not None
    assert await queued_jobs(engine, task="score_cycle", queueing_lock="score_cycle:score_now") == 1   # one request, not two
    with patched(generate_mod, "afault_point", PausePoint("score.generate.before_draft")) as pause:   # the cycle runs, blocked mid-target
        cycle = asyncio.create_task(run_queued_cycle(engine))
        await pause.reached.wait()
        r3 = await logged_in_client.post(f"/api/jobs/{job_id}/score-now", json=body, headers=idem("tab-3"))
        assert r3.status_code == 409 and r3.json()["code"] == "conflict_state"      # its target is in_progress
        r4 = await logged_in_client.post(f"/api/jobs/{other_job_id}/score-now", json=body, headers=idem("tab-4"))
        assert r4.status_code == 202                                                # another job: recorded, no second cycle
        pause.resume(); await cycle
    assert await count_rows(engine, "score_cycles") == 1 and (await cycle_report(engine))["claim_rounds"] == 2
    assert await charged_attempts(engine, pending_target) == 1 and await charged_attempts(engine, other_target) == 1
```

`tests/integration/test_api_activation.py`: activating prompt-schema v2 on an install whose pointer is v1 (the test inserts v1 as active and v2 inactive, surviving the reset) without a live dry-run is 409 `schema_dry_run_required`, and so is one whose live run covers the generate model but not the revise model (Codex r1 #25); with an inserted passed live run covering every (schema, model) pair and the gate uncalibrated it answers 201, flips the pointer, creates the tuple, records a settings version whose `effect_classes` holds the forced `llm_tuple`, cancels the old tuple's targets, opens the bulk operation **after** `_FLIP` (the fault point `settings.after_llm_tuple` sees the old pointer, `activation.after_cancel` the new one) and records `create_targets_for_tuple` (Codex r1 #35); a replay with the same key returns the stored 201; with 30 cases and no golden run it is 409 `eval_gate_failed` unless `eval_override_ack`; the rubric form the same on the rubric pointer; the listings show `active` and `dry_run`. `tests/integration/test_api_batches.py`: the release route on a `submit_ambiguous` submission (C21's user half), 409 otherwise, 422 without confirm, and the `Idempotency-Key`. `tests/integration/test_api_golden.py`: the feedback route's 201 and 409, the listing's cursor, the PATCH's canary limit, the DELETE's cascade, 422 on a NUL comment. `tests/integration/test_api_alerts.py`: the ack lifts `pause_reason` (a cycle starts afterwards), 404 on a resolved alert. `tests/integration/test_spend_route.py`: the four M3 fields with and without history. `tests/unit/test_openapi_m3.py`: every path above is in the generated document and the drift check passes.

### Task 23: Retention of the score tables, target-set deletion, and the `cycle-` checkpoint threads

**Files:**
- Modify: `backend/src/jobhunter/privacy/retention.py` (`RULES`, `_DELETES`, the thread rules), `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/targets.py` (`delete_target_set`), `backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/batches.py` (`UNRESOLVED_SUBMISSION_SQL`); update `tests/integration/test_retention.py` (the rule order pin)
- Create: `tests/integration/test_retention_m3.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `RetentionRule(name, window, depends_on)`, `RULES`, `_DELETES`, `_ENDED_THREADS`, `_PROFILE_THREADS`, `THREAD_BATCH = 500`, `_DELETE_EVAL_RUNS`, `run_retention_daily(engine, queue, saver, *, cv_store, now, epoch)` (`privacy/retention.py:148–656`); `Settings.retention.score_history_months` (§13.1 L4865, default 12; `settings/model.py:471`); `targets.cancel_targets`; `bulk.release_target_hold` (Task 10); `lock_active(share=True)`.
- Produces (§20.1 L6304–6305, L6308, L6312, L6320; §20.2 L6367–6378; B R13, Deviation 31):
  - `async targets.delete_target_set(conn, target_ids: Sequence[int], *, now) -> int`, the nine steps of L6369–6378 verbatim for one chunk of at most 200 targets, in one transaction: 1. the non-terminal targets of the set → `cancelled('purged')` (`in_progress` claims cleared, their attempts `cancelled`; `in_batch` targets cancelled individually, as a merge does (S:L3979): `batch_submission_id` cleared, their attempts `cancelled`, the submission untouched; every cancelled target's follow-up hold released, `bulk.release_target_hold`, Task 10, with `bulk.lock_for_targets(conn, target_ids)` taken before the first target-row lock, P52). S:L6370's "the submission's cancel is requested first" disagrees with S:L3979; M5, whose purges are the callers that meet `in_batch` targets (retention deletes only terminal ones), decides it for purges; 2. `resume_draft_id := NULL`; 3. `result_evidence` and `det_results` of the set's results (the `score_feedback` and `golden_cases.labelled_from_result_id` references become NULL by their FKs); 4. `critic_notes` of the set's attempts; 5. `llm_results` of the set (the deferred keys with `published_result_id` are satisfied because step 9 runs in the same transaction); 6. `score_drafts` of the attempts; 7. `batch_items` of the set with their `llm_calls` (the deferred pair), then every other `llm_calls` row of the attempts (the ledger rows stay); 8. `score_attempts`; 9. the targets. M5's purges reuse it.
  - The retention rules, in `RULES` after `eval_runs` and before `checkpoints`, in this order with these dependencies (Codex r1 #37, #38, #39): `score_history` (window `score_history_months` months, read from the active settings at the start of the pass): targets of **inactive** tuples that ended terminally (`published_* | refused | failed_* | expired_unscored | cancelled`) longer ago than the window (`state_changed_at`), excluding results referenced by an active golden case (`golden_cases.labelled_from_result_id` with `active`) and excluding targets that an **unresolved submission** still needs (Codex r4 B11): `batches.UNRESOLVED_SUBMISSION_SQL` = `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM batch_items i JOIN batch_submissions s ON s.id = i.submission_id WHERE i.target_id = t.id AND (s.state IN ('preparing', 'submit_started', 'submit_ambiguous', 'submitted', 'ended') OR (s.state = 'released' AND s.submit_started_at > now() − interval '29 days')))` (one clock, `submit_started_at`, the reconciler's: a released submission is listed for 29 days from it, L3984, Deviation 27; Codex r5 B9), negated in the rule's predicate, so a superseded tuple's terminal targets whose submission is `submit_ambiguous` keep their `batch_items` and `llm_calls` (the item-to-ledger mapping the release and the reconciliation join through, `spend_ledger.py:1097–1107`) until the submission is resolved by the reconciler or the user's release, and age out in the pass after that (the same predicate, the same 29-day tail as the `batch_submissions` rule below; no timeout releases anything, C3), deleted in chunks of 200 through `delete_target_set`, each chunk re-checking under `active_config FOR SHARE` that the tuple is still inactive (L6367); superseded `det_results` instances have **no rule of their own**: they go with their result in step 3 of `delete_target_set` (L6305 "with score history; the current instance follows its result"), so an old superseded instance beneath a retained active-tuple or golden-labelled result stays; `cycle_threads` (14 days after the cycle ended, L6320): checkpoint threads named `cycle-<id>` whose cycle `finished_at < now − 14 d`, in batches of `THREAD_BATCH` (the `cycle-` prefix never matches `_PROFILE_THREADS`' `profile-%` or `queries-%`, B R17), found through the cycle row as `_ENDED_THREADS` finds run threads; `score_cycles` (13 months after `finished_at`, L6312, `depends_on=("cycle_threads",)`, and only cycles whose thread has no row left in any saver table, so a cycle row never disappears before its checkpoints are gone: the `runs`/`checkpoints` pattern of `retention.py:181–184`); `batch_submissions` (a rule §20.1 does not list, Deviation 31): submissions in a terminal state (`reconciled | submit_rejected | abandoned`, or `released` with `submit_started_at` older than 29 days, so the ambiguous reconciler has stopped listing them: its clock, L3984) whose `batch_items` are all gone (deleted with their targets by `score_history`) and whose terminal timestamp (`reconciled_at`, `released_at`, `submit_started_at` for a rejection, `created_at` for an abandonment) is older than 13 months; `bulk_operations` (13 months after `finished_at`, L6312, `depends_on=("batch_submissions",)`, only operations no submission references any more). `eval_case_results` and their calls go with `eval_runs` (M2's rule; the cascades of Task 1). Every rule keeps M2's error boundary and `retention.failed`.
  - `golden_cases` exemption (L6307), **a contract for M5** (Codex r1 #44): the retention registry has no `tombstone` rule (`retention.py:163–185`; M1 deferred tombstoning), so nothing executable lands here. The contract M5's rule must implement: a closed or stale job is not tombstoned while any active golden case names its current revision or its profile version (`AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM golden_cases g JOIN job_revisions r ON r.id = g.job_revision_id WHERE r.job_id = j.id AND g.active)`), and a tombstoning that would orphan an **inactive** case deletes that case first (L6307); the same clauses apply to M5's purges. Recorded in Deviation 31 and the M5 Carry-forward; no M3 test exercises it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** `tests/integration/test_retention_m3.py`: a terminal target of an inactive tuple older than 12 months is deleted with every dependent row (the nine steps, its superseded and current instances, the ledger rows kept, feedback detached), while one of the active tuple, one younger, and one whose result an active golden case labels are kept, each with its superseded instances however old (Codex r1 #39); a chunk whose tuple became active meanwhile is skipped; `delete_target_set` on a set with a non-terminal follow-up target cancels it `purged` and releases its hold once; `cycle-` threads go 14 days after the cycle ended and `run-`/`profile-` threads are untouched; a cycle whose thread deletion failed in one pass keeps its row and both go in a later pass (Codex r1 #38); a populated batch history (two reconciled submissions, one released 40 days ago, one abandoned) ages out at 13 months after its items went, then its bulk operation, while a `submit_ambiguous` one and a `released` one of 20 days are kept with their operation (Codex r1 #37); `test_an_unresolved_submission_keeps_its_targets_through_retention` (Codex r4 B11): a superseded tuple's terminal targets, 14 months old, whose items belong to a `submit_ambiguous` submission of the same age survive the pass with their `batch_items` and `llm_calls`, the user's `release` afterwards moves every item's entry `dispatched → ambiguous` through the join on `llm_calls` (which still resolves) with the counted spend unchanged (L3997: the reservations stay counted; nothing is credited), the next pass deletes the targets (the submission's `submit_started_at` is 14 months old, so no listing window protects them any more), then the submission 13 months after its `released_at` (Codex r5 B9); the pass raises `retention.failed` on a failing chunk and continues.
