**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `quota/dryrun._arbeitnow(settings) -> (warnings, projection)` (`quota/dryrun.py:121–126`: `page_cap = min(10, max(3, ceil(interval_h / 30) + 1))`, `expected_pages = interval_h / ARBEITNOW_HOURS_PER_PAGE + 1`); `settings/effects.FIELD_EFFECTS` (`display.timezone` → `_NONE`, `:72`); `save._apply_schedule` (`settings/save.py:243–260`: `anchor_utc_for(a.now, schedule.anchor_local, a.proposed.display.timezone)`); `docs/spikes/V8.md` (page 1 holds 300 items and spans 02:32–20:00 on 2026-10-18, about 17.5 h; page 2 reaches back 53 h; every response is an edge-cache hit with a TTL of about one hour; the page cap stays).
- Produces (U3; M2 Deviation 39 and the M2 final review's two spec inconsistencies closed; Deviations 32 and 33):
  - `ARBEITNOW_HOURS_PER_PAGE: Final = 17.5` (V8's measured yield of page 1, the only page whose span the cap formula bounds; the old 37 came from an assumed yield) with the docstring and §6.8 L1109 changed to `expected_pages = proposed.interval_h / 17.5 + 1   # page 1 ≈ 17.5 h of postings (V8)`. The cap formula is unchanged (V8's decision). The warning now fires above 35 h within §13.1's 1–168 h: not at 24 h (2.37 expected pages, cap 3) or 35 h (3.0, cap 3), and at 36 h (3.06, cap 3), 48 h (3.74, cap 3), 72 h (5.11, cap 4), 120 h (7.86, cap 5) and 168 h (10.6, cap 7).
  - `display.timezone` takes the effect class `schedule` (`FIELD_EFFECTS["display.timezone"] = _SCHEDULE`), and §13.1 L4820 reads `| Display | \`display.timezone\` | IANA zone; display, and the zone of \`schedule.anchor_local\` | \`Europe/Berlin\` | schedule |`. Justification (U3 asks for one): the anchor is computed from the timezone at save time (L4818), and `_apply_schedule` already re-anchors from `proposed.display.timezone`; the class makes a timezone change run that existing action and tells the user, in the dry-run's effect list, that the schedule moves. Re-anchoring on every save would re-anchor on saves that touch neither field, and would re-run the dead-man update (§17.7) for nothing.

     followed by the **rules of the wire format** (new text after the block): *"Only stated facts are emitted; an absent code means `unspecified`, not asserted, or `required = false`. `value` carries the vocabulary word for `work_mode` (`remote | hybrid | onsite | unspecified`), `remote_region` (`germany | dach | eu_eea | europe | worldwide | other_country | unspecified`), `seniority` (`intern | working_student | apprentice | junior | mid | senior | lead | principal | head | unspecified`), `employment_type` (`full_time | part_time | contract | temporary | internship | working_student | apprenticeship | freelance`) and `degree` (`none_required | vocational | bachelor | master | phd | unspecified`); the integer text for `min_years_experience`; an ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code for `remote_country` (one item per country); the language code (`de | en | fr | es | it | nl | pl | pt`) or `other:<name>` for `required_language`; the name for `certification`; the industry for `industry`; the requirement text for `must_have` and `nice_to_have`; and `""` for `onsite_only`, `work_permit`, `security_clearance` and `degree_or_equivalent`. `qualifier` carries the CEFR level (`A1`–`C2`, `native`, `unspecified`) of a language, the field of a degree and the level of a clearance, and `""` otherwise. `required` is the fact's `mandatory`, `asserted` or `required` flag. A single-valued code appears at most once (the first is kept, V4); `degree_or_equivalent` sets the degree's `or_equivalent`. `profile_evidence[].section` names a profile section (`summary | target_titles | roles | skills | languages | education | certifications | domains | seniority`); an unknown section fails grounding (V3). The validator normalises this wire form into the internal draft of §11.4's fact table before V1–V6 run; the internal draft, not the wire form, is stored in `score_drafts.output_json` and rendered to the critic and the reviser (§10.6)."* The limits table (L4330–4347) gains the rows `| \`criteria\` | exactly one item per criterion key; a missing key is a V2 failure on that criterion, a duplicate keeps the first (V4) |`, `| single-valued fact codes | at most one item each (the first is kept, V4) |` and `| \`remote_country\` items | ≤ 30 |` (replacing the `remote_region.countries` row), and the preamble (L4209) adds: *"The API also enforces documented limits (24 optional parameters, 16 union parameters, 20 strict tools) and an undocumented compiled-grammar size with a 180 s compile timeout; `messages.count_tokens` does not compile the grammar, so only a message call proves a schema compiles (V4, V4b)."* (A3 G2.)
  2. **§11.4 (L4157–4172)**: the fact table gains a column `Generator code` (`work_mode`, `onsite_only`, `remote_region` + `remote_country`, `required_language`, `must_have` / `nice_to_have`, `seniority`, `min_years_experience`, `industry`, `employment_type`, `degree` + `degree_or_equivalent`, `certification`, `security_clearance`, `work_permit`, and `—` for `not_a_job_ad`, which is the top-level flag).

None of these is a structural failure."*; L4361 (V2) adds *"A fact without a fact id fails on its code (`work_mode`, `remote_region`, `seniority`, `min_years_experience`, `industry`, `employment_types`) or on `must_have:<n>` / `nice_to_have:<n>`."*; L4368 adds *"the recomputed rating keeps basis `observed` with the `seniority` and `min_years_experience` facts' quotes as its ad evidence, and becomes `unknown` when both sub-ratings are unknown"*; L4371 adds *"two items of a single-valued fact code: the first is kept"*; the repairs table (L4382–4392) gains `| An out-of-vocabulary \`value\` or \`qualifier\` | Treated as \`unspecified\` (the fact is not asserted) |`.   26. **§3.3.3 L312** (the global lock order): the schedule of Deviation 38 written out in its eleven steps, with the sentences *"A transaction that already holds a lock may name it again at any later step; a shared helper never takes an earlier step's lock for the first time after a later one. `ranking_state` is always the last lock. An API transaction stamps no epoch; the ones that take the month row take it after the configuration and the idempotency row and before any target row. The batch preparation is the one transaction that locks target rows after the month row: it does so with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and therefore never waits for a target. A transaction that creates its owner row inserts it after the rows its foreign keys reference have been locked, because an insert key-shares its parents. An operation's failure hook holds the operation row already and takes no earlier lock; cleanup that needs the configuration runs in a fenced transaction of its own afterwards. The epoch claim commits alone; every takeover sweep is then a transaction of its own that begins with the fence and the configuration, so no sweep holds another's rows."* (Codex r1 #18–#20, r2 B7, B8, r3 B1, B2, r4 B1).

Expected: HTTP 200 and `end_turn` for all four; a generator rejection would be a stop (the plan's premise, V4b, says it compiles on Sonnet). Central ≈ $0.06–0.07 actual; worst ≈ $0.24.

Central < $0.001; worst ≈ $0.012. `docs/spikes/V12.md` holds the result.
  3. **`v5`** with V14 folded in (§22 L6657, L6666; M2 Deviation 9): `scripts/spikes/v5_stream_timing.py` sends 20 streamed calls at effort `medium` through the real gateway in the recorder's `--through-gateway` mode: 10 generator calls (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, the C schema) and 10 critic calls (`claude-opus-5-5`), as 5 + 5 **identical-prefix pairs** rendered by `render.score_prompt` (the real three-block layout with the cache marker on `system[1]`, Task 4; a synthetic rubric block and profile block whose prefix is ≥ 4,000 tokens at chars/4, a different synthetic ad per pair; the second call of each pair within the 5-minute TTL), one call at a time, each recorded with its SSE event timestamps from `TimingTransport`; it measures per call the duration (sent to settled), the largest gap between SSE events ("pings < 30 s apart during thinking"), `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens`; acceptance: p99 (the maximum of 20) duration < 300 s, every gap < 30 s, the second call of each pair has `cache_read_input_tokens ≥ 4,000` (V14); the first generator call includes the grammar compile (A3 G28: it is reported separately, not excluded). **The budget covers every HTTP attempt** (Codex r1 #40): `SPIKE_CALLS["v5"] = 20` counts attempts, each authorised and reserved by `BudgetedTransport` before it leaves, so a transport retry of the gateway (up to six per logical call, `gateway.py:752–787`) consumes the allowance instead of exceeding it, and a refused attempt is answered with the synthetic 400 that the gateway does not retry; a retried call reduces the measured sample, which `docs/spikes/V5.md` reports. The recorder refuses the 21st attempt of `v5`; the stop rule: the script stops at the first refusal and `docs/spikes/V5.md` records what was measured. Central ≈ $0.66, high ≈ $0.83, worst ≈ $4.4 (`v14` + ≈ $0.08 / $0.6). Every recorded stream is committed under `generate/`, `critic/` (and the synthetic streams of Task 4 stay for the shapes they cover); `tests/payload/test_recorded_bounds.py` checks `input_tokens_bound ≥` the recorded usage of each.
  4. The ledger `.fixtures-raw/anthropic-budget.json` after the spikes: every entry done; the settled total copied into `docs/spikes/V19.md`'s spend table (expected about $0.9 of $10.00).

`test_a_failing_sweep_ends_the_takeover_and_the_next_start_sweeps_again`: a sweep that raises leaves the epoch claimed and the earlier sweeps' commits in place, `claim_epoch_and_sweep` raises, and the next start claims the next epoch and completes every sweep.

  - `tests/crash/test_score_worker_crashes.py` on the real worker (`jobhunter worker` children with `JOBHUNTER_FAKE_LLM=1`, the fixtures and a stalling fake where named): **C9** (`JOBHUNTER_FAULT=gateway.after_dispatch` kills the worker mid-stream: the entry is `ambiguous` after `llm_ledger_unsettled` at the injected deadline, the target is reclaimed by the second worker's takeover, the new attempt rebuilds from the rows, and the zombie's publish, replayed by hand with the old claim, affects 0 rows); **C14** (`JOBHUNTER_FAULT=claims.beat:sleep=200` stalls the heartbeat task during a 400 s stalling call: `stuck_targets` reclaims at 3 min, the new attempt completes, the original call's publish is rejected by the CAS, and the cycle ends `completed`, never `failed_invariant`); **C17** (the zombie worker of M0's harness with a cycle in flight: the old process's domain writes affect 0 rows, its checkpoints stay on its thread, the new attempt completes); **C29** (`JOBHUNTER_FAULT=score.claim.after_claim` kills the worker: the takeover sweep fails the cycle `orphaned`, reclaims its targets, and the next tick starts a new cycle); SIGTERM during a cycle gives `worker_shutdown_complete` with exit 0 and the in-flight entries `ambiguous`.
- [ ] **Step 2: Write the smoke script** as in Produces, importing M2's helpers; `test_smoke_m3_script.py` checks the check list and that it never prints a cookie or a key.
- [ ] **Step 3: Bring up the stack and run everything**

```bash
timeout 60 sh -c 'until docker info >/dev/null 2>&1; do sleep 1; done'
uv run ruff check . && uv run ruff format --check . && uv run mypy && uv run python scripts/check_llm_boundary.py
uv run --group docparse pytest -W error -q && uv run pytest -W error tests/payload -q && uv run pytest -W error tests/contract -q && uv run pytest -W error tests/eval -q
uv run --group docparse pytest -W error -m docparse tests/docparse -q
uv run pytest tests/stack -m stack -q && uv run pytest tests/perf -m perf -q
(cd frontend && npm test && npm run build && npm run lint && npm run typecheck) && scripts/check-openapi-drift.sh
docker compose --env-file deploy/images.lock -f deploy/compose.dev.yaml -f deploy/compose.fixtures.yaml -f deploy/compose.fakellm.yaml down -v
docker compose --env-file deploy/images.lock -f deploy/compose.dev.yaml -f deploy/compose.fixtures.yaml -f deploy/compose.fakellm.yaml up -d --build --wait
uv run python scripts/smoke_m3.py
uv run jobhunter schema-dry-run      # the fake replays the live v2 streams of Task 26: every gating case passes, exit 0
```

Expected: every command exits 0 (the dry-run included, which flips M2's expected failure: the generator case now passes on prompt-schema version 2), and the smoke script prints fifteen `{"check": …, "ok": true}` lines. No paid call runs in this step.
- [ ] **Step 4: Close the spikes and the roadmap.** This step is a gate:
  - `docs/spikes/V4.md` (the v2 section), `V4b-generator-v2.md`, `V5.md` (the paid part), `V12.md`, `V14.md` and `V19.md` (the M3 re-count and the final spend table) hold their results;
  - `.fixtures-raw/anthropic-budget.json` shows every entry done and a settled total below $10.00, copied into `V19.md`, with no key and no content;
  - `bounds.PROMPT_OVERHEAD_TOKENS` holds the re-measured values and `validate.overrun_buffer_bound` of the seeded settings is at most $2.00;
  - the spec is revision 3.5 (Task 25) and its `test_spec_revision` passes.
  Then set the M3 status cell of the roadmap to `complete`, and leave the other rows unchanged.
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit, then the final 4-eyes review**

```bash
git add scripts/smoke_m3.py tests docs/spikes docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-job-hunter-roadmap.md
git commit -m "test(m3): scoring end to end on the fake LLM through the graphs, the batches and the worker; acceptance smoke; mark M3 complete"
```

Then comes the milestone 4-eyes review of the roadmap's quality gate 3: a whole-branch review by Codex GPT-6 Astra (`model_reasoning_effort="high"`, never `xhigh`) and Claude Fable 5.1. Findings are fixed and the review repeated until both approve. Record it in `docs/council/m3-final-review/`, as M2's was.

## Deviations

Each deviation stages a spec item across milestones, or settles something the spec leaves open. Entries that change user-visible behaviour say so. Revision 3.5 of the spec (Task 25) carries every one that changes spec text.

1. **Tables staged across milestones** (continuing M0 Deviation 1, M1 Deviation 1 and M2 Deviation 1). M3 creates the 15 tables of §7.11–7.13 that M1 and M2 left, plus `score_cycles` of §7.6, which no milestone had created and which `score_attempts.cycle_id`, §7.16.8 and §7.17 #10 need (A1 issue 1). It closes the five FKs M2 staged, restores the NOT NULL of `active_config.rubric_version_id` and `prompt_schema_version_id` (M0 Deviation 1, M2 Deviation 32), and creates both §7.18 views. Nothing is staged beyond M3. User-visible: no.
2. **DDL beyond the spec text** (Task 1). Three CHECKs the spec leaves out (`transient_failures >= 0`, `spent_usd >= 0`, `repetition BETWEEN 1 AND 3`; A1 issues 4, 12, 29) — not `spent_usd <= budget_usd`, because a billed settlement above its reservation must be counted in full even at a full allocation (L1164; the admission predicate enforces the bound, the audit and the bound-violation alert report an overrun; Codex r1 #28); `eval_case_results.outcome` keeps the spec's five values (the `in_progress` step store of rounds 1–3 is withdrawn with the eval resume, round-4 ruling (a), Deviation 29); `prompt_schema_versions.parser_family`, the explicit parser family stored with a version (round-4 ruling (b), Deviation 40); `llm_results.critic_injection` (the recomputation of confidence needs the last review's assessment, Codex r1 #11); `batch_submissions.operation_id` (the recovery of a lost execution, Codex r1 #23); `batch_items.hold_usd` and `hold_released_at` (the once-only hold release, Codex r1 #27); `batch_submissions.operation_claim_id` (the execution that prepared the submission, Codex r2 B12) and `follow_on_at` (the once-only follow-on, Codex r2 B15); `critic_injection` admits `unavailable` (the critic ran and left no valid review, Codex r2 B17); `score_attempts.lane`, the lane snapshot of the admission that made the attempt, for the §9.6.5 alerts, with the same seven values as `score_targets.lane` (`always_include` included), both CHECKs written as one SQL literal list in 0008 (a migration imports no application code), pinned against `admission.LANE_VALUES` by `test_lane_values_pin_both_checks` (Codex r3 B15, r4 B2; the `parser_family` CHECK likewise, pinned against `schemas.PARSER_FAMILIES`); `current_scores` excludes `synthetic_eval` jobs (Codex r1 #32); `llm_spend_ledger.admitted_buffer_usd` so §7.17 #6's row inequality replays after a buffer edit (M2 Carry-forward; A1 issue 28); `jobs.synthetic_eval` and `profile_versions.synthetic_eval` for the adversarial golden-case rows (Deviation 21); and one index on each FK column that a retention or purge delete reaches through its referencing side (A1 issue 41; M1's note 9 precedent), including `det_results_result` for the cascade from `llm_results` that the partial `det_results_current` cannot serve. The `deferred_group_id … ON DELETE SET NULL` of L2230 cannot fire while a `deferred_duplicate` target names the group (the CHECK of L2244); every group deletion first dissolves the group, which moves its deferred targets to `pending` (Task 15), and M5's purges keep that order (A1 issue 5). User-visible: no.
3. **Layout differences from §4.2.** New modules: `scoring/{facts,det_results,steps,routing,targets,cycles,batches,golden,eval_runner,publish,contested,similarity,reconcilers}.py`, `graphs/score/{deps,context,claims,llm_step}.py`, `graphs/collect/report.py`, `dedup/group_rules.py`, `worker/embedding_reconcile.py`, `settings/activate.py`, `api/routes/{targets,golden,alerts}.py`, `llm/schemas/published.py`, `cli_golden.py`, `scripts/spikes/{v12_batches,v5_stream_timing}.py`, `tests/support/{scoring,batches,eval}.py` and `tests/eval/`. `labelling.py` of §4.2 stays M4's. User-visible: no.
4. **The generator output schema is candidate C of spike V4b, shipped as prompt-schema version 2** (U1, R12; M2 Deviations 17 and 33 closed; A3 G1, G2, G34, S1, S2; Task 4). The API rejects revision 3.4's §11.5 schema and candidates A and B ("The compiled grammar is too large"); C compiled on `claude-sonnet-5-5` (`docs/spikes/V4b-generator-v2.md`). The wire schema becomes generic fact items and generic criterion items; the validator normalises it into the §11.4 fact table and the six criteria before V1–V6, so §11.4, §11.7, §11.8, `llm_results.criteria`/`job_facts` and `result_evidence.subject` are unchanged; the internal draft is what `score_drafts` stores and what the critic and the reviser see. `profile_extract` and `propose_queries` stay byte-identical; version 1 keeps its row and hash. `PROMPT_OVERHEAD_TOKENS` is re-measured against the v2 requests (free); the overrun-buffer bound follows and the seeds stay unless it passes $2.00, which stops the task (M2 Deviation 34's rule). The nullable `level` stays an `anyOf` (the SDK refuses a typeless `enum`). §11.5's preamble names the API's documented limits and that `count_tokens` does not compile. User-visible: the output shape of the generator is internal; results look the same.
5. **Seeding and verification of the shipped versions** (Tasks 3, 4, 11, 20, 21; continuing M2 Deviation 32). `seed_defaults` inserts rubric v1 and v2 and prompt-schema versions 1 and 2 on every `migrate`; a NULL pointer (a fresh install) is set to the **newest shipped** version of each; a set pointer is never changed, so an upgraded install (none exists yet, M2 Deviation 34) keeps v1 until the user activates v2 through the route, which requires a passed **live** schema dry-run of that version (M2 Carry-forward 1) and the eval gate. Because scoring on an unverified schema would burn attempts, the one admission guard of the score lane (`cycles.pause_reason`, read by the cycle trigger **and** by the batch prepare; Codex r1 #25) pauses with `"schema_unverified"` while `validate.schema_verified` is false for the active version and the active model policy — a passed **live** dry-run case for each (schema, model) pair the policy uses, from any run of that version — unless the worker runs on the fake (dev and tests); production runs `jobhunter schema-dry-run --live` once after install (the runbook entry of Task 27). Compilation is proven per model, so an `llm_tuple` save that moves the generator, the reviser or the critic to a model without a passed live case for the active version is refused (`schema_dry_run_required`; A3 G30), the prompt-schema activation route applies the same check, and the dry-run CLI can run a candidate policy's cases. User-visible: the pause reason in the health section and on `/api/system/spend`, and the 409.
6. **Rubric v2** (R13; A3 G9; Task 3). §11.3's language anchor (L4129) and §11.4's table (L4197) disagreed on a mandatory language with an `unspecified` level that the profile holds; §11.4's deterministic rule wins (B2 or higher `met`, below `uncomparable`), the anchor text follows it, and the changed text is rubric v2 with a new `content_hash`; v1 stays as data. A fresh install seeds v2 active (Deviation 5); an upgraded install activates it through `POST /api/settings/rubric-versions/{id}/activate` (a re-score). User-visible: the rubric block the generator sees; a question for the user below.
7. **Target creation and revival, claim columns and `override_at`** (R4, R8; A1 issues 7–10, 21, 30; A2 R3; Task 14). A revived target starts a fresh life: `charged_attempts`, `transient_failures`, `not_before`, `resume_draft_id`, `refusal_category`, `state_reason`, `lane`, `override` and `override_at` reset, `bulk_operation_id` from the reviver; its guard (the tuple active again, the revision current, the job unmerged and not fenced) is the caller's, in the same transaction. Leaving `in_progress` clears `claim_id`, `claim_epoch` and `claim_heartbeat_at` and keeps `claimed_at`; every edge that sets `override` sets `override_at` (the CHECK of L2247). A job without a `hard_filter_results` row for the active scope gets it computed inline at creation. §7.17 #11 is tested on `state_changed_at` for revived targets. **The attempt limit outside `in_progress`** (Codex r1 #21): R6 charges a batch attempt at `submit_started`, so a rejected or ambiguous submission can leave a `pending` target with `charged_attempts = 4`, and a budget denial inside a fourth attempt can leave a `budget_deferred` one; §7.16.3 has no edge for them. Two edges are added, `{pending, budget_deferred} → failed_attempts_exhausted('attempts_exhausted')`, taken by the fourth admission sweep (`exhaustion_sweep`, Task 13, also run by the batch handler); the claim, the admission candidates and the batch candidate predicate all carry `charged_attempts < max_score_attempts`, so no path admits a fifth attempt; Retry (`charged_attempts := 0`, L2880) re-opens such a target. User-visible: a revived target (a reverted profile, an unmerge) may be scored again with four attempts; a target whose attempts were spent by batch rejections ends `failed_attempts_exhausted` and shows Retry.
8. **A bulk operation completes only when its `create_targets_for_tuple` operation has ended and none of its targets is `pending`, `in_progress` or `in_batch`, and no submission of it is in flight** (R5; A1 issues 18, 19; Task 14). `budget_deferred` and `deferred_duplicate` targets do not hold it open; when they return to `pending` after completion, their reservations carry no `bulk_operation_id` and debit the bucket. User-visible: `/api/system/spend`'s `bulk_operation` ends a little later than the last batch.
9. **Node 9 and I5 use the run's tuple** (R9; A2 R1, R2; B R5; Task 14). A run creates targets for `runs.llm_tuple_id` while it is still the active tuple, re-checked under `active_config FOR SHARE` per chunk; I5 is evaluated against the run's tuple and passes with a recorded reason when that tuple was superseded during the run (the confirm's `create_targets_for_tuple` owns the new tuple). Node 9 cancels superseded revisions' unpublished targets of the run's tuple only. User-visible: no false `failed_invariant` after a mid-run confirm.
10. **`batch_submissions.reserved_usd` and `followup_held_usd` are `numeric(12,6)`** (R7; A1 issue 11; A2 B6): the ledger and bulk amounts they sum are `numeric(12,6)`. User-visible: no.
11. **A set-based transition helper** (R3; A1 issues 13, 14; Task 2). §7.16's "every state change MUST go through `transition(table, id, …)`" is kept for single rows; the atomic claim, the revive upsert, the bulk cancellations, `budget_release`, the sweeps and target-set deletion use `transition_where` over the same registry, validated the same way, and the `assert_transition` trigger checks every row. `batch_submissions` has no reason column in the registry (its `reconcile_report` is JSONB, written by `set_`), a changed-at column only for the states that have one, and the CAS on `state` as its predicate; `batch_items` uses the submission's predicate through the owner lock. A cast inside a static statement is always `CAST(:name AS type)`, never `:name::type`, which `sa.text` does not bind (Codex r2 B2). User-visible: no.
12. **`score_attempts.outcome` is a registered machine, and the batch outcomes the spec leaves open are mapped** (A1 issue 15; A2 B10; Task 2): `running → ` each of the 13 terminal outcomes; an abandoned submission ends its attempts `cancelled('batch_abandoned')`; a `retry_online` decision ends the batch attempt with the outcome its cause maps to (`transient_error` for `batch_errored | batch_canceled | batch_expired | batch_missing`, `failed_max_tokens` for `batch_max_tokens`, `failed_invalid_output` for `batch_invalid_output`); an `unexpected_stop_reason` item is `failed_invalid_output`; an `ignored_fenced` item whose attempt is still `running` ends it `cancelled('ignored_fenced')`. User-visible: no.
13. **A batch attempt is charged at `submit_started`** (R6; A1 issue 16; A2 B9; Task 7): the step-2 transaction that marks the item entries `dispatched` sets `charged` on every attempt and `charged_attempts + 1` on every target, which §7.11 L2286 defines as "one LLM call was dispatched". A rejected or ambiguous submission therefore counts against the four attempts; an abandoned one does not. User-visible: a target of a rejected batch has one attempt fewer.
14. **`user_job_actions` is M3's, with the merge, unmerge and tombstone wiring** (R1; A1 issue 2; M1 Deviation 7 closed; Task 22). The seen/saved/applied/hidden/note routes stay M4's. User-visible: merged jobs keep their actions; a re-posted hidden ad stays hidden.
15. **The ledger's `bulk_operation_id` is set only on the reservations of batched targets and their funded follow-ups** (A2 B2; Task 7). §6.9.2 L1173 and §6.9.3 L1187/L1193 are read together: a batched target's item and its follow-ups debit `spent_usd` while they fit, and an entry that would not fit is stored without the id and debits the bucket; online targets of the operation always debit the bucket. The budget is fixed at the operation's start even across a month boundary (A2 R5). User-visible: no.
16. **The graph shape the spec leaves implicit** (A2 G1, G2; Tasks 9, 11). `TargetOutcome` (`target_id`, `attempt_no`, `outcome`, `state`, `lane`, `cost_usd`) and the generalised `merge_outcomes`; the deterministic nodes' timeouts (60/120/120/60 s) beside the LLM nodes' `TimeoutPolicy`; the subgraph's edges as L3546–3564 with Deviation 17's settlements. User-visible: no.
17. **Settlements inside the per-target path** (A2 T1, T2, T3, T5, T6, T8, T9, T11, T12, T15, T17; Tasks 9, 10): `generate` stores the draft with the normaliser's diagnostics in `validation` and placeholders in the other NOT NULL columns, which `validate` fills, writing the **corrected** draft back into `output_json` so every later reader and a resumed attempt see the V4 corrections (Codex r1 #6, #9); the error exits of L3551 apply to `critic` and `revise`; a tuple whose prompt-schema version cannot score (`parser_family = 'generator_v1'`) ends the attempt `transient_error` without a charge (`PromptVersionUnscorable`, Deviation 40); `not_a_job_ad` is published `true` only when verified by a confirmed, grounded review, in both publication modes (Codex r1 #10); a structurally invalid revision after the repair publishes `contested` from the last validated draft (R5's "never withheld") while a failed generator still fails; a reclaim's new attempt pins the then-active execution config and evaluation policy (L3491) and an in-process resume keeps the attempt's; the structural repair is per attempt; `llm_calls.round` is the draft's round for `generate`/`revise` and the critic round for `critic`; `logical_call_no = score_attempts.logical_calls + 1`; `InputUnbounded → failed_request('input_unbounded')`, `LLMNotConfigured → transient_error` without a charge, `ModelMismatch → failed_request('model_mismatch')`; a `claim_lost` attempt is ended by whoever changed the predicate (a reclaim writes `claim_lost`, a cancellation `cancelled`) and the coroutine never writes after a lost claim; `fail` raises `llm.cap_reached` under the month lock after the owner row; `load_context` reads the previous attempt's last call (or `batch_max_tokens`) to start enlarged, and restores, over the whole attempt chain of the target under the same tuple and policy (`resumed_from_attempt_id`), the newest draft, the critic-round count and the last valid review's derived outcome, confirmations and not-a-job-ad verification, which `validate` then routes by instead of calling the critic again — an accepted review publishes, a round-0/1 `revise | incomplete` review revises, a round-2 one publishes contested, a draft newer than every review goes to the critic (L3523's "no completed LLM work is repeated" over L3548's `critic_rounds > 0 → critic`; Codex r2 B23, r3 B8, B9); a contested publication after two structurally invalid critic outputs records `critic_injection = 'unavailable'`, read as "not `none`" by the confidence rule (Codex r2 B17). User-visible: a contested result where revision 3.4 would have failed the target.
18. **Two alert codes §17.6 lacks** (A2 S5, S6; M2 Deviation 6's pattern; Tasks 10, 11): `scoring.publication_invalid` (critical, L4394's "plus a critical alert") and `score_cycle.failed` (critical, §17.1's "cycle failed, critical alert"). M5's catalogue adds them. User-visible: the alerts.
19. **Where the score lane pauses, and how it resumes** (M2 Deviation 11 closed; A2 T16; B R11; Tasks 11, 13, 18, 21). The pause is checked at the cycle trigger (`cycles.pause_reason`), never inside a running cycle: an **unacknowledged** `llm.reservation_bound_violated`, an **unacknowledged** `llm.auth_failed` (the same `acknowledged_at IS NULL` predicate in the guard, Task 11, since a paused lane makes no scoring try that could auto-resolve it; Codex r7 B5), the current month's scoring headroom below `r_first` (the `cap_reached` reason reads the ledger, never the once-per-month `llm.cap_reached` alert, whose marker is never cleared and whose stale rows of earlier months `budget_release` resolves at every tick; Codex r5 B4), a global purge fence, or an unverified schema (Deviation 5). `POST /api/alerts/{id}/ack` is pulled into M3 to lift the first two. User-visible: the pause reason, and the ack route.
20. **Cycle rulings** (A2 G3–G11, G13, A3 (ĉ), A7; B R4; Task 11): `ĉ` averages online attempts with a dispatched call that ended published under the active tuple and policy, with at least 20 (else §21.2's central); a `score_now` or `batch_followup` trigger during a running cycle is served by that cycle's next claim round (the `more` edge returns to `admit`, which in a later round admits the urgent targets only and the claim takes that round's list, at most 5 rounds; the lane on the target is the record, and the waiting `score_cycle` job finds nothing left or a cycle still running: Deviation 39; Codex r1 #14); every claim's heartbeat is started by the cycle right after the claim commits (`ClaimHeartbeats.start(ref)`; a claim waiting for a fan-out slot is never reclaimed while the cycle lives), the `score_target` node of the parent is a wrapper that runs the subgraph in an owned child task, attaches it to that heartbeat (`attach(ref, child)`, the one API the declaration, the callers and the spec amendment share; Codex r3 B18), turns ownership loss into a `claim_lost` outcome (never a graph error that aborts the siblings) and projects the outcome into `results` (Codex r1 #12, #13; r2 B5, B6); `S = 0` for a `score_now` cycle, the bucket-gated `S` for `batch_followup` (its follow-ups are always-include candidates and count against `S` and the bucket, L3600, while their reservations debit the allocation), `S = max(0, …)`; the cycle row's `S`, `ĉ` and `bucket_usd_at_start` are computed at insert; the cycle records the policy active at its start and each attempt pins the one active at its claim (`ĉ` is keyed by the cycle's); the claim transaction starts with `fence_epoch`; `finalize_cycle` resolves `score_cycle.orphaned` and evaluates `scoring.critic_rate_high`; the claim and every sweep that moved a target bump `ranking_state`; the periodic trigger runs in its own fenced transaction after `run_tick` (it needs the month row, which `run_tick`'s `schedule_state` lock would precede). User-visible: no.
21. **Golden cases** (R2; A1 issues 3, 24, 26; A3 G22; Tasks 19, 21). M3 ships `POST /api/jobs/{job_id}/feedback`, `GET` and `DELETE /api/golden-cases` and a `PATCH /api/golden-cases/{id}` `{active?, is_canary?}` (nothing else writes those two fields; at most two canaries); the labelling UI is M4's. `DELETE` deletes the row and its eval rows without the `golden_case_delete` manifest, which needs M5's manifests. A `label` feedback is `source = 'week1'` and a `wrong_score` feedback `'feedback'`. Adversarial cases are ten committed synthetic fixtures (`backend/src/jobhunter/scoring/eval_fixtures/adversarial/`, packaged in the wheel for the dev stack: §18.6's eight bullets with the injection pair as two files and both seniority traps of L4610; Codex r1 #43) that `jobhunter golden seed-adversarial` loads as synthetic jobs, revisions, profile versions and scope versions marked `synthetic_eval`, which get no target, no embedding (the `awaiting_embedding` scan excludes them), no row in either §7.18 view (`NOT j.synthetic_eval` in `current_scores`; Codex r1 #32) and are retention-exempt while their case is active. User-visible: the routes and the CLI.
22. **`r_first` and `ĉ_followup`** (A2 A9, S7; Task 7): `r_first` is the worst-case first-stage reservation of a generator call at §21.4's component sizes and the measured overhead (a fixed figure, since each ad's bound differs); `ĉ_followup` is the critic and revision parts of §21.2's per-target cost divided by the first-round critic rate ($0.066939 for policy B, the §21.4 figure). User-visible: no.
23. **Routes** (R10; B R15, R18; Task 21). M3 ships the activation routes (with the live dry-run gate and the eval gate; an activation flips the pointer and runs an internal `llm_tuple` save, which records the override and calls the one shared `activate_tuple`), the two version listings (so the id to activate is discoverable), score-now, retry, the manual cycle, batch release, feedback, golden cases, alert acknowledgement and the M3 fields of `/api/system/spend`; `POST /api/duplicate-groups/{group_id}/score-individually` and everything else in §14.2 stay M4's. User-visible: the routes.
24. **Admission rulings** (A2 A1, A2, A4, A5, A6, T18, R7, G10; A1 issue 20; Tasks 12, 13): always-include takes at most `⌊S / 2⌋` similarity slots (the property test L5697 governs L3635's `ceil`; at `S = 1` always-include waits, and Score now bypasses); the claim records `always_include_reason`, and batched targets are identified through `batch_items`, never `lane`; `max_targets_per_cycle` and `min_cycle_targets` (class `budget`) take effect at the next cycle; the `admission` save sweeps immediately in its own transaction; `expired_unscored → pending` clears the reason; the scope flip's `filtered_out → pending` honours `override`; Score now's 409 is a headroom check against `r_first` made before the target is touched, from an unlocked read of the month row (the attempt's reservation is the authority), and the score-now transaction never takes the month row, so it cannot wait on `budget_release` (Codex r1 #19); a slot-backed candidate the claim then skips has cost its lane one credit (as L3650 has it); a later claim round re-admits the urgent targets rather than re-claiming an earlier list (Codex r1 #14). User-visible: at `S = 1` an always-include target waits for `S ≥ 2`.
25. **Deterministic-layer rulings** (A3 G10, G11, G14, G23, G24, G25, G31, G32; Tasks 3, 6): the degree ordering treats a profile with only `other` degrees as `uncomparable` and an empty education list as `none`; `none_required` is no requirement; the fold of `other` names and certification names is `normalize.text.fold`; T4's 60 is the constant `T4_MIN_MID` (mandatory, never a policy field); the cap of 25 and the penalty of 10 are code constants whose change bumps `DET_ALGORITHM_VERSION`, which stays `"2"` for M3 (no `det_results` row predates it); soft penalties are deduplicated per code with the industry preference as the code `industry`, and `industry_match` is `excluded` or `outside_include` only from a non-structured fact (a structured one is the filter's); `outside_include` **is** produced, from a non-empty `industries_include` (§13.1 L4827, `settings/model.py:310`), with `excluded` taking precedence and an empty list meaning no preference (the first draft's claim that §13.1 has no include list was wrong; Codex r1 #5); the five scoring fields of the scope (`industries_include`, `language_policy`, `language_policy_default`, `min_salary_eur_year`, `knockout_severity`) join `ScopeSettings`, so one view serves the filters, the knockouts, the criteria and the hashed `det_inputs` (Codex r1 #4); `employment_salary` compares the highest stated EUR figure (`annual_eur_max`, else `annual_eur_min`); `location_work_mode` stays `null` when the possible modes disagree. User-visible: the salary rule for minimum-only ads, and a configured industry preference now lowers the score of ads outside it.
26. **Validator rulings** (A3 G3, G12, G13; Codex r1 #6, #7, #8, #10, #43; Task 5): a missing criterion key in C's array is a V2 failure on that criterion (never a parse failure, L4353), and the normaliser's issues, failures and repairs are returned beside the draft, stored with it and merged into stage 2, so they survive a resume; V2's quote requirement applies to every asserted fact, keyed by its fact id, by its code for an id-less fact, or by `must_have:<n>` / `nice_to_have:<n>`; the recomputed `experience_seniority` keeps basis `observed` with the seniority and years facts' quotes and becomes `unknown` when both sub-ratings are unknown; the internal draft's `ProfileRef.section` is a string so that an unknown section is V3's failure (`profile_section_unknown`), not a structural one (the strict publication model keeps the literal); grounding maps a quote through the block's origin map, so a tail quote lands on the right description span, and a quote over 300 characters is a V3 grounding failure of its criterion or fact before routing and the null-out (L4387), not a V1 issue that publication would cut silently (Codex r3 B12); the review of `not_a_job_ad:ad` is special-cased (`fact_rejected` → `revise` as a real ad, `uncertain` or missing → `incomplete`, only a confirmed grounded review verifies the label); V5's patterns are spelled as regular expressions that accept the spec's qualifiers in any number and order (`ignore all previous instructions` hits). User-visible: no.
27. **Batch rulings** (A1 issues 17, 22, 23, 32; A2 R4, R6, B1, B3–B5, B7, B11–B18; Tasks 14, 16, 17): the `in_batch` targets of a superseded tuple are cancelled in the transaction that records the cancel request on **every** non-terminal submission of the tuple, whatever its phase (so no late item can pass the fence, and a batch created after the request is cancelled as soon as its id is known for a batch still in progress: `record_created` or `batch_poll`; an adopted candidate has ended already and its items are `ignored_fenced`; Codex r1 #24, r7 B3); `submit_start` re-checks the tuple, the bulk operation and the cancel request and abandons instead of dispatching an obsolete submission; `canceled` items of that submission are `ignored_fenced`; every item decision but `followup` releases its hold when processed through the once-only `batch_items.hold_released_at`, a `followup` when its target ends terminally, and the `bulk_operations` reconciler releases the rest at completion (Codex r1 #27); the submission's owner row is the spend owner and the operation's claim only executes it, and the submission records its `operation_id` so a lost execution's `preparing` row is abandoned and its `submit_started` row made `submit_ambiguous` at takeover, on the re-run and by the `batch_submissions` tick reconciler (never by a failure hook, which could not take `active_config` after the operation row), never resent (Codex r1 #23, r3 B2, r4 A); a succeeded item's message is classified exactly as an online response (model mismatch, `max_tokens`, refusal, structural validity, storability), its usage always billed and its output stored only when valid (Codex r1 #26); the prepare consults the same admission guard as the cycle trigger, verifies the operation's claim, sizes the batch under the month and bulk rows, takes its targets by a `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` CAS that advances `attempt_no` before any dependent row exists, re-applies the minimum to the claimed set and rolls a short batch back whole, and `reserve_batch` re-checks the allocation under the locks it holds (Codex r1 #18, #25; r2 B8–B10); a never-dispatched funded reservation credits the bulk debit when released (r2 B11); the submission stores the preparing execution's claim, which the recovery predicate compares with the operation's current claim and epoch, so recovery holds at takeover whatever the sweep order (r2 B12, r3 B3); recovery runs in fenced transactions of its own (the takeover sweep, the re-run handler, the `batch_submissions` tick reconciler), never in an operation failure hook, which would take `active_config` after the operation row (r3 B2); the prepare takes the month and bulk rows before it inserts its submission row, whose FK key-shares the bulk row (r3 B1); every cleanup ends an attempt only while it is `running` and settles accounting regardless (r2 B13); a missing item goes through `process_item` as a `missing` result, in a transaction of its own after the completion marker committed and before the reconcile transaction, never inside the submission's transaction (r2 B14, r6 B1); the follow-on is keyed by the originating submission and marked once on it (r2 B15); `reserve_batch` reserves every item under one month lock; `batch_submit_rejected` settles the calls `error`; `create` runs under a 600 s client deadline; a byte cap of 200 MiB beside `batch_max_items`; "younger than 29 days" counts from `submit_started_at`; a submission "ends" in `reconciled | released | submit_rejected | abandoned`; `batch_poll` and `batch_reconcile_ambiguous` are periodic aux tasks (the reconciler framework has no 5- or 15-minute cadence); missing items' entries move `dispatched → ambiguous`; the workspace is assumed to be this installation's alone; a follow-up re-validates under its own attempt's policy; `batch_min_targets` governs L3708's "≥ 200". User-visible: the batch cancel reaches the provider within five minutes of a confirm.
28. **Reconciler and alert rulings** (A1 issues 27, 29; A2 A8, S4; Task 18): `stuck_targets` reclaims only targets whose attempt is still `running` (a fenced publish's target waits for the purge); `budget_counters` also audits each active bulk operation's `spent_usd` against the ledger; the admission alerts' windows are the trailing 7 days of attempts for `admitted_per_day` and the similarity-lane admissions' current `similarity` for the percentile, with the lane of an admission read from `score_attempts.lane` (the snapshot of Task 1), never from the mutable `score_targets.lane` (Codex r3 B15); `scoring.stuck_target_repaired`'s subject is the hour; §7.17 #4 is read with §8.7's eligibility list. User-visible: no.
29. **Eval rulings** (A1 issue 25; A3 G15, G20, G21, S3; B R9, R14; Tasks 19, 20): `tuple_identity` is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the four tuple ids; Spearman uses average ranks for ties over published cases, `failed` outcomes count against schema validity, filtered and refused cases are excluded from ρ and the pairwise agreement, the test–retest sample is the ten lowest-id active cases with a numeric score in both repetitions, every golden run scores it twice, and a run that cannot measure it (fewer than five usable cases) is `failed`, never passed by omission (Codex r1 #33); expected-knockout recall is vacuous for week-1 cases; the gate reads the newest completed golden run of the candidate's (rubric, prompt/schema, model policy, evaluation policy), and with 30 or more active cases and no run it is `failed` (a paid run, or `eval_override_ack`, is needed); the canary baseline key is (case, rubric, prompt/schema, model policy, evaluation policy); `projected_critic_rate` is replayed at a policy row's creation and fixed for the row (an identical payload reuses its row and rate); eval runs reuse the scoring steps and book every call non-scoring with `golden_case_id`, their paid calls and domain writes are owned by the operation's claim as well as the run row (a reclaimed execution is fenced; Codex r1 #29), **a run is scored by one execution and never resumed** (round-4 ruling (a), replacing the durable step store and receipt replay of Codex r1 #30, r2 B19, r3 B4, B5, which produced findings in every round — r4 B4–B7 — because a replay has to reproduce the production routing and every receipt state): the steps of a case live in the execution's memory, its only durable write is its terminal row, an execution that finds the run already touched (a call row or a result row) ends it `failed` with `metrics.failed = "interrupted"` and succeeds its operation in the same transaction, an execution that finds it already terminal only succeeds its operation, the failure hook of an operation that ends `failed` fails the run only while it is still `running`, the run's completion and its operation's end commit together (`op.finish_in`; Codex r5 B2), and the user starts a new run, at the cost of one run (about $2.70 for a golden run); every gate metric is computed over one observation per active case (its first repetition), the test–retest SD over the paired repetitions and the cost over every row, so the adaptive retests can change no gate (r4 B10); the run records the transport requested and served in `metrics` (the table has no reason column), a worker of the other kind fails it before any call, and the gate accepts fake provenance only in a fake-mode process (r2 B20, r3 B6); a candidate without rows is staged by content hash through `ensure_derived` under `active_config FOR UPDATE`, the lock that serialises `version_no`, without activation (r2 B21, r3 B7); the manual workflow starts a live-configured worker for the operation it queues (r3 B13); the second repetitions are adaptive in case-id order up to twenty attempts and the sample is read from the results (r2 B22); eval publication goes through the pure `publication.build`, never the target transaction (r2 B18), and a run is abandoned when its operation is lost or fails, not after a fixed hour (the hour stays for claimless CLI probes; Codex r1 #31); `eval-manual.yml` is the adversarial smoke on a throwaway database (candidate `active` only, gate state `uncalibrated` by construction) and the golden evaluation runs on the installation through the CLI, which refuses to dispatch without resolvable ids and active cases (Codex r1 #34); the C35 test helper registers under kind `export`. User-visible: the gate's `failed` state without a run, the smoke's scope, and an eval run interrupted by a worker crash or takeover ends `failed` ("interrupted") and must be started again instead of finishing on its own.
30. **Merge and unmerge details** (R1; §8.6.1 steps 1, 3, 4; §8.6.2 steps 4, 5; Task 22): the snapshot holds both action rows and the from-job's non-terminal target ids; the from-job's `in_batch` targets are cancelled individually (`job_merged`) without cancelling the batch; the unmerge's revival goes through `create_or_revive`; the note cut at 20,000 characters keeps both full notes in the snapshot. User-visible: the merged note's separator line.
31. **Retention of the score tables is M3's** (B R13, R17; A2 G12; Codex r1 #37–#39, #44; Task 23): the §20.1 rows for score history (12 months after the terminal end, inactive tuples only, active golden cases' results kept; superseded instances go with their result, never by their own age; targets whose batch item belongs to an unresolved submission are kept with their items and calls until the submission is resolved (a released one counts as unresolved for 29 days from `submit_started_at`, the reconciler's clock, Codex r5 B9), so the release and the reconciliation keep the item-to-ledger mapping they join through, and no age releases a submission, C3; Codex r4 B11), the `cycle-` checkpoint threads (14 days) before the cycle rows (13 months, a row only after its thread is gone), batch submissions (a rule §20.1 lacks: 13 months after they ended, once their items went with their targets) before bulk operations (13 months), with target-set deletion implemented for retention and reused by M5's purges; the roadmap gave §20 to M5, but a running score lane would otherwise grow without bound. The tombstone rule and its golden-case exemption stay M5's (no tombstone rule exists yet); Task 23 records the exemption contract M5 must implement. User-visible: no.
32. **The Arbeitnow coverage-risk warning uses V8's page yield** (U3; M2 Deviation 39 closed; Task 24): `ARBEITNOW_HOURS_PER_PAGE = 17.5` (page 1 spans about 17.5 h of postings in V8's recordings) replaces the assumed 37; the cap formula stays. User-visible: the warning fires for intervals above 35 h (none at 24 h or 35 h; a warning at 36, 48, 72, 120 and 168 h).
33. **`display.timezone` has the effect class `schedule`** (U3; Task 24): a timezone change re-anchors the schedule through the existing `schedule` action, and the dry-run lists it. User-visible: the effect class shown for a timezone change, and the re-anchored schedule.
34. **Spikes** (U2; M2 Deviation 9; A3 G26–G29; Task 26): a fresh live dry-run of version 2 (`v4v2`), V12 on Haiku with the real C schema, V5's 20 calls as identical-prefix pairs with V14 folded in (10 generator + 10 critic), an optional two-call `v14` pair for the same-model two-schema question, each with its own count and the $10.00 actual stop rule; an expired batch item cannot be produced on demand, so that decision is tested on the fake only; V5's p99 of 20 samples is the maximum, with the first call's grammar compile reported separately; the gateway spikes run in the recorder's `--through-gateway` mode, where a budgeted transport authorises, reserves and counts **every HTTP attempt** before it leaves (the allowance counts attempts, a refused attempt is answered with a local 400 the gateway does not retry) and a timing transport captures the SSE timestamps and the exact three-block request, asserted offline before the live run (Codex r1 #40, #41). Every recorder run unsets `LANGSMITH_API_KEY` for its own process. User-visible: no.
35. **Worker wiring** (B R6, R10, R12, R23; Task 27): four new tasks (`score_cycle` on `score` with `lock="score"`, `batch_poll`, `batch_reconcile_ambiguous` on `aux`, `canary_daily` on `tick`); `OPERATION_LOCKS` gives every enqueue and re-enqueue of a `batch_submit` operation `lock="batch"` (derived from the kind inside `defer_operation_job`, so a job the `operations` reconciler re-defers keeps it; Codex r3 B20); the epoch claim commits alone and the sweeps then run in registration order, each in a fenced transaction of its own that takes `active_config FOR SHARE` before any operation, owner or domain row, so the `operations` sweep's `FOR UPDATE` rows are never held while another sweep asks for the configuration, whichever sweep is registered first (the one-transaction-per-sweep option of the round-4 ruling; `claim_epoch_and_sweep` is split accordingly and a failing sweep ends the takeover as today; Codex r4 B1); the pools keep the spec's sizes and a perf test measures a 60-target cycle; `budget_release` and `stuck_targets` run on the minute tick and the rest on the heavy pass; the `_ADMISSION` split (M2 Carry-forward I3); a lone stalled `score_cycle` job is failed, not retried (`NO_STALL_RETRY`, since the inherited rule retries first and the task never resumes a cycle; Codex r1 #17); `eval_run` gets a failure hook (a failed run failed, inside the framework's finishing transaction, which takes no earlier lock; Codex r1 #31), while `batch_submit` gets none: its recovery needs `active_config` and runs in the fenced `batch_submissions` tick reconciler instead (Codex r1 #23, r3 B2). User-visible: no.
36. **Test harness and fakes** (R11; B R2, R7, R8; Codex r1 #1, #2; Tasks 1, 4, 8, 19): keyed fake answers (`X-JH-Fake-Key`, fake mode only) and a scripted fake Batches API; `reset_database` drops `jobs`, `job_revisions`, `duplicate_groups` and `operations` (reached through `batch_submissions.operation_id`; Codex r2 B1) from the truncate set (a `TRUNCATE … CASCADE` follows the FK graph whatever the rows, and `score_targets → llm_calls → profile_drafts → profile_versions → active_config` would be truncated) and deletes them with the M3 tables, a test pinning that no truncated table reaches the configuration; the v1 prompt-schema hash is a pinned literal; the configuration reset points at the newest shipped versions; tests of "ship, then activate" insert their own inactive version with its `parser_family`; the eval-run test helper moves to kind `export`. User-visible: no.
37. **Cost-model notes** (A3 G6, G7, G8, G16–G19; §21; Task 25): §21.4's sizing is restated at the measured overhead and the v2 schema size; §21.1 names the structured-output framing tokens; §10.6 notes that cache entries are per (model, effort, schema), which V14 measures and which makes preset A's shared entry an estimate; the arithmetic notes of G16–G18 stay as they are (judgements, not errors); the canary cost of §21.3 ($3.80 with cache writes) is the figure the plan uses. User-visible: the projection figures only.
38. **One lock schedule for the score lane** (Codex r1 #18, #19, #20; Global Constraints; Tasks 7, 10, 13, 14, 16, 17, 18, 21). §3.3.3 L312 orders `active_config` → job rows → owner → month → … but says nothing about the fence, the idempotency row, the bulk row or a shared helper's locks; the plan writes the eleven-step schedule out, makes every transaction's steps explicit at its task (every batch transaction, `submit_start`, the three recordings, `abandon`, `recover_lost` and `release` included, takes `active_config` before the operation and submission owners; `process_item` and the publish take `active_config` and the job before the owner; `activate_tuple` opens the bulk operation under the month row before it touches targets, and the save's and the confirm's `ranking.bump` follow it so `ranking_state` is always last; the publish releases an item hold at the bulk row before its domain inserts; `score_now` never takes the month row; no API transaction stamps an epoch, and the two that take the month row take it in schedule order; the batch prepare is the one stated exception that locks targets after the month row, through `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so it never waits for a target, and it inserts its submission row only after the month and bulk rows, because the row's FK key-shares the bulk row; an operation failure hook takes no earlier lock, so cleanup that needs the configuration runs in a fenced reconciler instead; the takeover's epoch claim commits alone and each of its five sweeps runs in a fenced transaction of its own that takes `active_config FOR SHARE` before any operation, owner or domain row — the one-transaction-per-sweep option of the round-4 ruling, chosen over a central acquisition in the shared transaction because the month row (step 6) cannot be held before the operation and submission owners (step 5) without inverting the order `prepare` and every attempt take them in, and because one transaction over five sweeps would hold one sweep's step-10 rows while the next takes its step-5 rows; Codex r2 B7, B8, r3 B1, B2, r4 B1) and pins the named pairs with `lock_wait_details`. Task 25 item 26 writes it into the spec. User-visible: no.
39. **A trigger requests a cycle; the worker creates it** (Codex r1 #16, #19, #20; Tasks 11, 13, 21). §9.3 L3493–3499 has the API "defer a `score_cycle`" and the one-running index decide; the first draft had the API insert the cycle row, which needs a worker epoch the API does not have and the month row the API should not take. Now every trigger defers `score_cycle(trigger)` (one waiting job per trigger kind, `lock="score"`), the task creates the row under `fence_epoch` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the one-running index, and the manual route answers `{queued, reason}`. User-visible: the manual route's body; a requested cycle starts a few seconds later than an inserted one would.
40. **The pinned prompt-schema version renders** (Codex r1 #2, #3; Tasks 4, 9, 19). §11.11's identity requires that what a tuple renders is what its version row holds; the renderer therefore loads `PromptVersion` (templates, schemas, `parser_family`) from the row by id and never from module globals, version 1 is frozen (its own template and schema constants, its hash pinned as a literal), **the parser family is an explicit constant stored with the version row** (`prompt_schema_versions.parser_family`, written by the seed, the only writer of version rows in M3, and pinned by a test against the literal values; round-4 ruling (b), replacing the structural canonicaliser of r3 B11, whose metadata stripping r4 B3 showed could not be made obviously correct — a stored schema that does not fit its family is caught by the gateway's mirror validation of every output and by the live dry-run before activation, not by inspecting the schema), so two stored versions of the same family with different texts both render and parse, the gateway takes the stored wire schema and the structural mirror as two inputs (`WireSchema`), and a version of family `generator_v1` makes an attempt `transient_error` without a charge while the `schema_unverified` pause keeps it from being scored on at all (Codex r2 B3). User-visible: no.
41. **Normalisation diagnostics and corrections are durable** (Codex r1 #6, #9; Tasks 5, 9, 17). §11.6 says repairs are "corrected and recorded" without saying where; the plan stores the normaliser's diagnostics with the draft at `generate`, writes the V4-corrected draft back into `score_drafts.output_json` at `validate` (the raw wire output stays on the call row), and merges the diagnostics into stage 2, so the critic, the reviser, the publication and a resumed attempt agree with the provisional score; the `validation` JSON keeps `normalisation` and `corrections` immutable (the first `validate` writes the replaced values once) and rewrites only `current`, so a re-validation or a reclaim never erases the audit history, and the pure publication layer rebuilds `truncated_fields` and the repairs from that history plus its own repairs, so a result published after a reclaim lists the same truncations as one published at once (Codex r2 B16, r3 B10). User-visible: no.

## Questions for the user

Questions 1–4 were answered by the user on 2026-10-26, each as recommended; the plan applies the answers as settled rulings. Question 5 stays provisional: the plan already applies its recommendation, and a different answer changes the named task.

1. **Rubric v2 (R13, Deviation 6).** The anchor fix makes rubric v2; the plan seeds v2 as the active rubric on a fresh install and leaves an upgraded install on v1 until the user activates v2 (a re-score). **Recommendation:** accept; no production database exists, so no re-score is ever paid for the fix. **Answered 2026-10-26: accepted.**
2. **Always-include at most `⌊S / 2⌋` (Deviation 24).** The property test's "never exceeds half of `S`" wins over `ceil(0.5 × S)`, so at `S = 1` an always-include candidate waits for a larger cycle. **Recommendation:** accept; Score now bypasses admission for anything urgent. **Answered 2026-10-26: accepted.**
3. **The eval gate without a run (Deviation 29).** With 30 or more active golden cases and no golden run for a candidate, the gate is `failed`, so a rubric, prompt-schema, model-policy or evaluation-policy change needs a paid golden run (about $2.70, plus the test–retest sample scored twice) or `eval_override_ack`. **Recommendation:** accept; the alternative (`uncalibrated` until a run exists) would let any change bypass the gate. **Answered 2026-10-26: accepted.**
4. **The unverified-schema pause and the per-model dry-run gate (Deviation 5).** Production scores nothing until `jobhunter schema-dry-run --live` has passed once for the active prompt-schema version on the active models, and an `llm_tuple` save that moves a node to a model never dry-run is refused until a live run covers it. **Recommendation:** accept; compilation is proven per model and a rejected run is free. **Answered 2026-10-26: accepted.**
5. **`DELETE /api/golden-cases/{id}` without its manifest, and the `PATCH` route (Deviation 21).** The delete lands in M3 without the `golden_case_delete` manifest (M5's manifests), and `PATCH {active?, is_canary?}` is the only writer of those fields. **Recommendation:** accept; the manifest is added in M5 with the other deletion manifests. **Answered 2026-10-26: accepted (with the plan approval).**
